@q pr1-09 | os-process#1 | 2 | 追蹤
行程 P 建立後依序：被排程執行 → 要求讀檔 → 讀檔完成 → 再被排程 → 時間片用完 → 再被排程 → 結束。P 總共**進入** ready 狀態幾次？
== 3
---
new→ready（建立後第 1 次）、waiting→ready（讀檔完成，第 2 次）、running→ready（時間片用完，第 3 次）。被排程是 ready→running，不算進入 ready。

@q pr1-10 | os-process#1 | 2 | 陷阱
Use the five-state process model (new, ready, running, waiting, terminated). A shell creates a process for the command `sort big.txt`, where `big.txt` is a 50 MB file. The kernel finishes setting it up and puts it into the ready queue; the scheduler soon gives it the CPU. The process asks to read `big.txt` and must wait about 8 ms for the disk. When the data arrives, the process goes back to the ready queue, is later given the CPU again, and runs until its 10 ms time slice expires. It is given the CPU once more, writes its output, and exits. Which of the following transitions occur during the life of this process? Select all that apply.
[x] new → ready
[x] running → waiting
[x] waiting → ready
[x] running → ready
[x] running → terminated
---
「new → ready」出現：建立完成、放進就緒佇列。

「running → waiting」出現：讀檔要等磁碟。

「waiting → ready」出現：資料到了，回到就緒佇列，而不是直接上 CPU。

「running → ready」出現：10 ms 的時間片用完。

「running → terminated」出現：寫完輸出後結束。五個都出現。

要記的是**不存在**的轉換：ready → waiting（要等 I/O 必須先在跑才能發出請求）、waiting → running（事件發生後先回 ready，由排程器決定何時再跑）。

@q pr1-11 | os-process#1 | 2 | 情境
Two web browsers are compared on the same machine with 20 tabs open. Browser S runs all 20 tabs as parts of one process and uses 1.2 GB of memory in total. Browser M runs each tab as a separate process and uses 2.0 GB in total; opening a new tab in browser M takes about 40 ms longer than in browser S. In a test, a buggy script makes one tab crash with an invalid memory access. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[x] In browser M, the crash does not bring down the other 19 tabs.
[ ] Browser M uses less memory than browser S, because each tab process loads only what it needs.
[ ] Tabs in browser M can exchange data faster than the parts of browser S can.
[ ] Tabs in browser M can communicate by reading each other's variables directly.
[ ] Opening a tab as a separate process costs no more than adding a part to an existing process.
---
「M 裡一個當掉不影響其他 19 個」對：每個行程有自己的位址空間。這是最主要的好處，用資源換隔離；在 S 裡，一個無效的記憶體存取會讓整個行程連同 20 個分頁一起結束。

「M 比較省記憶體」錯：每個行程有自己的位址空間和程式狀態，比較耗；量到的是 2.0 GB 對 1.2 GB。

「M 交換資料比較快」錯：同一個行程裡直接共用記憶體最快；跨行程要經過作業系統。

「直接讀對方的變數」錯：位址空間是分開的，溝通要經過作業系統提供的機制，例如管道、共享記憶體、訊息。

「開新分頁不會比較貴」錯：要建立新的位址空間等資源，量到每次多約 40 ms。

@q pr1-12 | os-process#1 | 3 | 計算
每次 context switch 花 0.2 ms，每個行程每次都用滿時間片。要讓 CPU 花在真正工作上的比例 ≥ 95%，時間片至少要幾 ms？
== 3.8
---
$\frac q{q+0.2}\ge0.95$ ⇒ $q\ge0.95q+0.19$ ⇒ $0.05q\ge0.19$ ⇒ $q\ge3.8$。時間片越大負擔越小，但互動回應越慢。

@q pr1-13 | os-process#1 | 1 | 比較
A user starts the same text-editor program twice, so the system lists two processes with IDs 501 and 502. The program file on disk contains 8 MB of machine code. After starting, each editor allocates about 30 MB of data for its own document, and the user types different text into the two windows. A memory report shows that the 8 MB of code occupies physical memory only once. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The system now has two processes running the same program.
[x] Each process has its own variables, because their address spaces are separate.
[ ] Text typed into process 501 automatically appears in the variables of process 502.
[x] The read-only code can be shared in memory by the two processes, which is why the 8 MB appears only once.
[x] The program is a passive file, while each process is that program in execution.
---
「兩個行程跑同一個程式」對：每次執行產生一個新的行程。

「各有自己的變數」對：位址空間獨立，各自的 30 MB 資料互不相干。

「501 打的字自動出現在 502」錯：變數不共用；要共享資料得透過作業系統提供的機制。

「唯讀程式碼可以共用」對：資料各自一份，程式碼可以只放一份。

「程式被動、行程主動」對：程式是硬碟上的檔案；行程是執行中的程式，有自己的記憶體、暫存器、狀態。

@q pr1-14 | os-process#1 | 1 | 計算
單核 CPU，系統有 5 個行程：1 個正在跑、2 個在等磁碟。ready 佇列裡有幾個？
== 2
---
$5-1-2=2$。單核任何時刻最多 1 個 running。

@q pr1-15 | os-process#1 | 2 | 觀念
記憶體不夠時，作業系統把整個行程搬到磁碟（swap out），等一下再搬回來。這是誰的工作？
[x] 中期排程器（medium-term scheduler）
[ ] 短期排程器（CPU 排程器）
[ ] 長期排程器（決定誰可以進入系統）
[ ] 行程自己
---
短期：每幾 ms 挑下一個跑的；長期：控制同時有幾個行程（批次系統）；中期：暫時把行程整個搬出去降低記憶體壓力，被搬出去的在 suspended 狀態。

@q pr4-19 | os-process#4 | 2 | 找錯
A student claims: "Whenever the running process receives an interrupt, it becomes waiting." Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] An interrupt alone does not uniquely determine which process runs after the handler.
[x] A timer preemption can move the former running process to the runnable queue.
[x] After another process's I/O-completion interrupt, the interrupted process may resume.
[x] A process blocked for its own unfinished I/O is in a different state from a runnable process queued for a processor.
[ ] Every interrupt necessarily terminates the interrupted process.
---
「中斷本身不唯一決定後續排程」對：處理器先處理事件，再由狀態與政策選擇。「計時搶先可回就緒」對：仍可執行，只是讓出 CPU。「別人的 I/O 完成後可續跑」對：不代表自己需要等該 I/O。「阻擋與就緒不同」對：前者缺事件，後者已能跑但等資源。「中斷必終止」錯：一般計時器與裝置中斷不等於致命錯誤或結束行程。

@q pr1-17 | os-process#1 | 2 | 計算
C 程式中的 `static int x = 5;`（函式外）、`int y;`（函式外、沒初始化）、函式裡的 `int z;`、`malloc` 拿到的空間，各在行程記憶體的哪一區？
[x] data、bss、stack、heap
[ ] stack、stack、stack、heap
[ ] data、data、heap、heap
[ ] text、bss、stack、data
---
text 放程式碼；data 放有初值的全域／static；bss 放沒初值的（載入時清成 0，檔案裡不用存）；stack 放區域變數；heap 放動態配置。

@q pr1-18 | os-process#1 | 2 | 情境
An 8-core database server answers queries slowly; each query now takes about 3 s to answer. A monitoring tool shows that CPU utilization is only 5%, that 190 of its 200 processes are in the waiting state, and that the disk is busy 100% of the time with a queue of about 60 requests. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[ ] The bottleneck is the CPU, so a faster CPU would help the most.
[ ] A faster CPU would make the waiting processes finish waiting sooner.
[x] Such a workload is called I/O-bound.
[ ] Faster disks would help less here than a faster CPU.
[ ] Low CPU utilization always means that there are too few processes.
---
「瓶頸在 CPU」錯：190 個行程都在等 I/O，磁碟一直滿載，CPU 只用了 5%，瓶頸在 I/O。

「CPU 快，等待就變短」錯：等的是磁碟，跟 CPU 速度無關。

「I/O-bound」對。

「加快磁碟不如加快 CPU」錯：剛好相反；更快的磁碟，或把常用資料放在記憶體，直接縮短等待。

「CPU 使用率低一定是行程太少」錯：這裡有 200 個行程，只是都在等。

@q pr1-19 | os-process#1 | 3 | 追蹤
單核、先到先跑（不搶先），A、B 都在時間 0 到，A 先。A：CPU 3 → I/O 5 → CPU 2；B：CPU 4（不做 I/O）。時間 7.5 時 A、B 各在什麼狀態？
== waiting,terminated
---
A 0–3 跑，3–8 做 I/O；B 3–7 跑完。7.5 時 A 還在等 I/O（waiting），B 已結束，CPU 閒著。A 8–10 跑完。

@q pr1-20 | os-process#1 | 3 | 計算
接上題，時間 0 到 10 之間 CPU 使用率是幾 %？
== 90
---
CPU 忙的時段：0–3（A）、3–7（B）、8–10（A），共 9；閒 7–8。$9/10$。如果 B 的 CPU 是 5，就剛好把 A 的 I/O 蓋滿，使用率 100%。

@q pr1-21 | os-process#1 | 3 | 證明
說明為什麼 context switch 時一定要把暫存器（包括程式計數器）存進 PCB，而不能只存「下一條指令的位址」。
---
行程被換下時，暫存器裡有算到一半的值（例如迴圈變數、運算中間結果）；換回來時要讓它以為自己從沒被打斷，所以所有暫存器、程式計數器、狀態旗標都要原樣還原。只存位址的話，下一條指令會拿到別的行程留下的暫存器值，結果錯誤。

@q pr2-11 | os-process#2 | 3 | 陷阱
```
printf("hi");
fork();
printf("\n");
```
（輸出到螢幕）`hi` 會出現幾次？
== 2
---
`"hi"` 沒有換行，還在 stdio 的緩衝區裡沒真正寫出去；`fork()` 把整個記憶體（含緩衝區）複製一份，兩個行程結束時各自把緩衝區寫出。程式在電腦上實際跑過，輸出 `hi` 兩次。

@q pr2-12 | os-process#2 | 3 | 陷阱
```
printf("hi\n");
fork();
```
直接在終端機跑 `hi` 出現幾次？改成 `./a.out > out.txt` 時 out.txt 裡呢？
== 1,2
?? 終端機,導到檔案
---
終端機是行緩衝，遇到 `\n` 就寫出，fork 前緩衝區已空；導到檔案時是全緩衝，`hi\n` 還在緩衝區裡被複製。實際跑過驗證。所以 fork 前常要 `fflush(stdout)`。

@q pr2-13 | os-process#2 | 3 | 計算
```
if (fork() || fork()) fork();
printf("x");
```
共印出幾個 x？
== 5
---
第一個 fork：父得非 0（`||` 短路，條件成立）→ 再 fork 一次；子得 0 → 做第二個 fork：其中父得非 0 → fork，子得 0 → 條件不成立。行程數：父那支 2 個＋子那支 (2+1) 個 = 5。程式實際跑過印出 5 個。

@q pr2-14 | os-process#2 | 3 | 比較
同上但把 `||` 換成 `&&`：`if (fork() && fork()) fork();`，印幾個 x？
== 4
---
第一個 fork 的子行程得 0，`&&` 短路，不再 fork（1 個）；父行程做第二個 fork：子得 0 短路（1 個），父再 fork（2 個）。$1+1+2=4$。實際跑過。

@q pr2-15 | os-process#2 | 2 | 計算
```
int x = 1;
fork(); x++;
fork(); x++;
printf("%d", x);
```
印出的數字是多少？共印幾次？
== 3,4
?? 數字,次數
---
fork 之後每個行程有自己的 `x`，互不影響。每個行程都做了兩次 `x++`，所以都是 3；共 4 個行程。實際輸出 `3333`。

@q pr2-16 | os-process#2 | 2 | 陷阱
```
printf("A\n");
execlp("nosuchcmd", "nosuchcmd", NULL);
printf("C\n");
```
`nosuchcmd` 不存在，輸出是？
== A C
---
exec 成功就不會回來；**失敗**時回傳 $-1$，繼續執行下一行，所以印 C。實際跑過。exec 後面通常緊接錯誤處理。

@q pr2-17 | os-process#2 | 2 | 觀念
子行程印 `C`，父行程先 `wait(NULL)` 再印 `P`。輸出順序？
[x] 一定是 `CP`
[ ] 一定是 `PC`
[ ] 可能 `CP` 也可能 `PC`
[ ] 只印 `P`
---
`wait` 讓父行程等到子行程結束。沒有 `wait` 時兩種順序都可能，看排程。

@q pr2-18 | os-process#2 | 2 | 情境
父行程 fork 出 3 個子行程，子行程馬上 `exit(0)`；父行程接著睡 60 秒，沒有呼叫 `wait`。這 60 秒內有幾個 zombie？
== 3
---
子行程結束了，但結束狀態還沒被父行程收走，PCB 留著：zombie。父行程結束後它們變成孤兒、被 init 收走清掉。

@q pr2-19 | os-process#2 | 2 | 找錯
想建立 3 個子行程各做一件事，學生寫 `for (i = 0; i < 3; i++) fork();`。問題？
[x] 子行程也會繼續跑迴圈，總共變成 8 個行程（多了 4 個）；子行程應該在 fork 回傳 0 時做完事就 `exit` 或 `break`
[ ] 沒問題，剛好 3 個子行程
[ ] 只會產生 1 個子行程
[ ] fork 不能放在迴圈裡
---
正確寫法：`if (fork() == 0) { work(i); exit(0); }`。總共 1 個父＋3 個子。

@q pr5-20 | os-process#5 | 3 | 情境
shell 執行 `ls | wc -l`，大致要呼叫幾次 fork、幾次 exec、建幾個 pipe？
== 2,2,1
?? fork,exec,pipe
---
先建 pipe；fork 第一個子行程，把 stdout 接到 pipe 寫端，exec ls；fork 第二個，把 stdin 接到讀端，exec wc。父行程（shell）關掉兩端、wait 兩個。

@q pr2-21 | os-process#2 | 3 | 情境
A server runs this loop:
```
while (1) {
    wait_for_request();
    if (fork() == 0) { serve(); exit(0); }
}
```
Each request is handled by a new child process, which exits as soon as it has served the request, and the parent never calls `wait`. The server handles about 50 requests per second; after about 10 minutes, `ps` shows some 30000 entries marked `<defunct>`, and new `fork` calls start failing because the process table is full. Several children may exit at almost the same moment, so their signals can arrive while a handler is already running. The developers consider several fixes before the next deployment, including calling `wait` once inside a `SIGCHLD` handler, calling `waitpid` in a loop there, or calling `wait` right after each `fork`, and they want the server to keep accepting new requests while old ones are still being served. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Each child that has exited stays a zombie until the parent calls `wait` or `waitpid` for it.
[x] Installing a `SIGCHLD` handler that runs `while (waitpid(-1, &st, WNOHANG) > 0) ;` reaps every child that has already exited.
[ ] Calling `wait(&st)` exactly once in the `SIGCHLD` handler is enough, because one signal is always delivered for each child that exits.
[ ] Calling `wait(&st)` in the parent right after each `fork()` removes the zombies without changing the server's behavior.
[ ] If the parent process exits, its zombie children remain forever, because no process can call `wait` for them any more.
---
「呼叫 wait 前一直是 zombie」對：子行程結束後只留下 PCB 裡的結束狀態，等父行程來讀；父行程不讀，就一直占著行程表。

「`SIGCHLD` 裡用 `WNOHANG` 迴圈」對：迴圈把所有已結束的都收掉；`WNOHANG` 讓沒得收時馬上返回，不會卡住伺服器。

「只呼叫一次 `wait` 就夠」錯：好幾個子行程幾乎同時結束時，同一種訊號在處理前只會記一次，一次 `wait` 只收一個，剩下的還是 zombie。

「fork 後馬上 `wait`」錯：父行程會等這個子行程處理完才接下一個請求，伺服器變成一次只服務一個人，行為完全改變。

「父行程結束後 zombie 永遠留著」錯：孤兒行程會被重新指定給 init（或 systemd），它會負責 wait。所以重開伺服器也會清掉 zombie，但沒有解決根本問題。

@q pr2-22 | os-process#2 | 2 | 觀念
父行程比子行程先結束，子行程會怎樣？
[x] 變成孤兒，被 init（或指定的收養者）收養；子行程繼續正常執行
[ ] 立刻被殺掉
[ ] 變成 zombie
[ ] 跟著暫停
---
收養它的行程之後負責 `wait`，所以孤兒不會永遠當 zombie。

@q pr3-07 | os-process#3 | 3 | 陷阱
In C, `errno` holds the error code of the most recent failed library function call, and a typical check looks like this:
```
if (read(fd, buf, n) < 0) {
    if (errno == EINTR) retry();
    else report(errno);
}
```
A program runs 8 threads on a 4-core machine, and each thread performs this check after its own `read` calls. A reviewer worries that a failing call in one thread could overwrite the value that another thread is about to test, and asks how C libraries handle `errno` in multithreaded programs, and whether a lock is needed. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[x] In a multithreaded program, each thread has its own `errno`.
[ ] Even if `errno` were one variable shared by all threads, a failing call in another thread could never disturb this thread's check of it.
[ ] Each CPU core has its own `errno`.
[ ] All threads share one `errno`, so every library call that may fail must be protected by a lock.
[ ] An ordinary global variable declared in the program gets a separate copy in each thread.
---
「每條執行緒各一份」對：支援多執行緒的標準函式庫把 `errno` 做成執行緒私有的變數。

「共用的話也不會被干擾」錯：共用時，別的執行緒的失敗呼叫可能在「自己失敗」和「自己檢查」之間把它蓋掉。這正是改成私有的原因。

「每個核心一份」錯：執行緒會在核心之間搬來搬去，綁在核心上沒有意義。

「共用一份、每個可能失敗的呼叫都要上鎖」錯：已經是私有的，不需要這樣做。

「一般全域變數每條執行緒一份」錯：一般全域變數是整個行程共享的；只有特別宣告成執行緒私有的才各一份，`errno` 是函式庫特別處理的。

@q pr3-08 | os-process#3 | 2 | 觀念
Two threads share the global variable `count`, initially 0, and each executes the loop below without any synchronization:
```
for (int k = 0; k < 1000000; k++)
    count++;
```
On the target machine, `count++` is compiled into three instructions: load `count` into a register, add 1 to the register, and store the register back to `count`. A thread can be interrupted, or can run on another core at the same time, between any two of these instructions. A student runs the program 10 times on a 4-core laptop and sees final values such as 1384211, 1999874, and 2000000. The student wants to explain these results and to find a version that always gives 2000000 without making the loop much slower, and compares several versions on the same laptop. A classmate predicts that the final value can never fall below 1000000. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The final value of `count` may be less than 2,000,000.
[x] `count++` is carried out as a load, an add, and a store, so one thread's update can be overwritten by the other's.
[ ] The final value of `count` is always at least 1,000,000.
[ ] The final value of `count` may be greater than 2,000,000.
[ ] If each thread instead adds into its own local variable and the two totals are added after both finish, the result may still be less than 2,000,000.
---
「可能小於 200 萬」對、「讀、加、寫三步會互相蓋掉」對：兩條執行緒都讀到同一個舊值、各自加 1 再寫回，兩次加法只剩一次的效果。

「至少 100 萬」錯：最壞可以只有 2。例如甲讀到 0 後被換下；乙做了 999,999 次；甲寫回 1（蓋掉乙的進度）；乙讀到 1 後被換下；甲做完剩下的 999,999 次；乙最後寫回 2。

「可能大於 200 萬」錯：每次寫回的值都是某次讀到的值加 1，更新只會遺失、不會多出來。

「各自累加仍可能不足」錯：區域變數在各自的堆疊上，不共享就沒有競爭；最後相加只做一次，在兩條都結束之後，一定是 200 萬。

@q pr3-09 | os-process#3 | 2 | 情境
多對一模型（多條使用者執行緒對一條核心執行緒）。其中一條執行緒呼叫會阻塞的 `read()` 讀網路。其他執行緒？
[x] 全部跟著停：核心只看到一條執行緒，它阻塞了整個行程就不能跑
[ ] 繼續跑
[ ] 被終止
[ ] 改由另一個 CPU 繼續
---
所以多對一模型通常要搭配非阻塞 I/O；一對一模型就沒有這個問題。

@q pr3-10 | os-process#3 | 2 | 計算
程式有 20% 只能單執行緒跑，其餘 80% 可以平均分給執行緒。用 4 條執行緒（4 核）最多快幾倍？無限多核呢？
== 2.5,5
?? 4 核,無限多核
---
$\frac1{0.2+0.8/4}=2.5$；核數無限時趨近 $\frac1{0.2}=5$。開更多執行緒不會無限變快，序列部分是上限。

@q pr3-11 | os-process#3 | 2 | 計算
多對一模型的行程有 3 條使用者執行緒，跑在 4 核心的機器上。同一時刻最多幾條在執行？改成一對一模型呢？
== 1,3
?? 多對一,一對一
---
多對一時核心只排程一條核心執行緒，只能用一個核心；一對一每條都能被核心排到不同核心。

@q pr3-12 | os-process#3 | 3 | 找錯
A program creates four worker threads and wants to give each one a different ID from 0 to 3:
```
pthread_t t[4];
for (int i = 0; i < 4; i++)
    pthread_create(&t[i], NULL, work, &i);
```
Each thread starts by reading `*(int*)arg` as its own ID and uses it to choose which quarter of a 1000000-item input to process. In testing, the program sometimes processes one quarter twice and skips another, and the results differ from run to run, even though the same input is used every time. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] All four threads receive the address of the same variable `i`.
[x] Two threads may read the same ID, and some ID may never be read, because `i` may change before a thread reads it.
[ ] Each thread is guaranteed to read a different value among 0, 1, 2, and 3.
[x] Passing `&id[i]` from an array `int id[4]` with `id[i] = i` gives each thread its own slot and fixes the problem.
[x] Making `i` a global variable does not fix the problem.
---
「都拿到同一個 `i` 的位址」對：`&i` 是迴圈變數本身的位址，四次都一樣。

「可能重複或漏號」對：新執行緒什麼時候真正去讀 `*arg` 不一定，可能主執行緒已經把 `i` 加了好幾次；迴圈結束後甚至可能讀到 4。

「保證 0～3 各一個」錯：如上。

「用 `id[i]` 陣列」對：每條執行緒拿到不同的位址，裡面的值也不會再改。另一種做法是把整數本身轉成指標傳值。

「改成全域變數沒用」對：問題是「大家共用同一個會變的變數」，放在全域還是共用一份。

@q pr3-13 | os-process#3 | 3 | 陷阱
兩條執行緒各執行一次 `g = g + 1;`（`g` 原本 0，共用，沒加鎖），主執行緒 join 兩條後印 `g`。可能印出哪些值？
== 1 2
== 1,2
---
正常是 2；但若兩條都先讀到 0、各自加 1 寫回 1，最後就是 1。join 只保證它們做完，不保證不互相干擾。

@q pr3-14 | os-process#3 | 2 | 情境
In a GUI program, all window events, such as clicks, key presses, and redraw requests, are handled by one user-interface thread that runs an event loop. Clicking "Export" makes that thread call a function that compresses 2 GB of data, which takes about 30 seconds, and the whole window freezes until it finishes: it cannot even be moved or redrawn. The machine has 4 cores and is otherwise idle, and the scheduler gives each thread a time slice of 10 ms. A developer proposes several fixes, and the team wants the one that keeps the window usable during the export. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[x] Moving the computation to a worker thread keeps the user-interface thread responsive.
[ ] The window freezes because the process's time slice is too short for the system to redraw it between computations.
[ ] Increasing the time slice of the process fixes the freezing.
[ ] The worker thread should redraw the window itself when it finishes, without involving the user-interface thread.
[ ] On a single-core machine a worker thread cannot help, because only one thread runs at a time.
---
「丟給工作執行緒」對：事件迴圈只有一條，它在算東西時就沒人處理點擊和重畫；計算移走後，介面執行緒就能繼續處理事件。

「時間片太短」錯、「把時間片調大」錯：問題不在排程，而在同一條執行緒同時被要求做兩件事；時間片大反而更不靈敏。

「工作執行緒自己重畫」錯：多數介面函式庫只允許介面執行緒動畫面；應該通知介面執行緒，由它更新。

「單核就沒用」錯：單核也會在兩條執行緒之間切換，介面執行緒仍能定期處理事件，畫面就不會凍住。

@q pr3-15 | os-process#3 | 2 | 比較
A web server handles up to 20000 requests per second, each needing about 2 ms of work. In design A, it creates a new thread for every request and destroys it when the request is done; in design B, it creates a fixed pool of 64 threads at start-up and hands each request to a free thread, while extra requests wait in a queue. During a promotion, the request rate suddenly jumps to 100000 per second for a few minutes. The developers compare the two designs before the next promotion. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] A thread pool avoids the cost of creating and destroying a thread for every request.
[x] A thread pool bounds the number of threads, so a sudden burst of requests cannot exhaust the system's resources.
[ ] A thread pool cannot serve several requests at the same time.
[ ] When there are more requests than threads in the pool, the extra requests must be rejected immediately instead of waiting.
[ ] Under heavy load, creating one thread per request always gives lower response times than a thread pool.
---
「省掉反覆建立／銷毀的成本」對：建立執行緒要配置堆疊、核心資料結構等，請求很短時這筆成本很顯著。

「限制執行緒數，請求暴增也不會拖垮系統」對：每條執行緒都要記憶體，無限制地開可能把系統資源耗盡。

「不能同時處理多個請求」錯：池子裡有幾條執行緒，就能同時處理幾個。

「多出來的請求立刻拒絕」錯：通常放進佇列等到有執行緒空出來；這是代價，但系統資源的上限可控。

「重負載時每請求一條一定比較快」錯：執行緒太多時切換成本、記憶體壓力反而讓每個請求都變慢。

@q pr3-16 | os-process#3 | 2 | 觀念
Two thread libraries are compared on a 4-core machine. In the 1:1 model, each user thread is backed by its own kernel thread, so the kernel sees and schedules every thread separately; in the N:1 model, all user threads of a process share one kernel thread, and a library inside the process switches among them. A program creates 4 threads that each compute for 10 seconds, and in a second test one of the 4 threads also reads from a slow disk. The developers measure how long each program takes under both models, and they want to know which model Linux pthreads use and how the Linux kernel treats the threads of a process. In the first test, the N:1 version takes about 40 seconds and the 1:1 version about 10 seconds, on an otherwise idle machine. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Linux pthreads use the 1:1 model.
[x] In the 1:1 model, threads of the same process can run on different cores at the same time.
[x] In the N:1 model, threads of the same process cannot run on different cores at the same time.
[x] In the N:1 model, if one thread waits for I/O inside the kernel, all threads of that process have to wait.
[x] In Linux, the kernel schedules each thread of a process individually.
---
「Linux 一對一」對：Windows 也是。核心執行緒建立成本夠低，一對一最簡單。

「一對一可以真的平行」對：核心看得到每條執行緒，可以分別排到不同核心。

「多對一不能平行」對：核心只看到一條核心執行緒，同時只能在一個核心上跑。

「多對一時一條等 I/O、全部都等」對：核心讓那條核心執行緒去等，其他使用者執行緒也跟著停。

「Linux 核心個別排程每條執行緒」對：一對一時每條都是核心排程的對象。五句都對。

@q pr3-17 | os-process#3 | 3 | 觀念
The C library function `strtok` splits a string into tokens: the first call passes the string, and later calls pass `NULL` and continue where the previous call stopped. A program uses it in 8 threads to parse log lines:
```
char *tok = strtok(line, " ");
while (tok != NULL) { handle(tok); tok = strtok(NULL, " "); }
```
With 1 thread the output is correct, but with 8 threads some tokens come from the wrong lines. A developer replaces `strtok` with `strtok_r`, which takes an extra pointer argument supplied by the caller, and the problem disappears. A reviewer asks the developer to explain why, and to state which kinds of functions are safe to call from several threads at once, for example a function that uses only its parameters and local variables, or one that keeps a static variable between calls. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] `strtok` remembers where it stopped in a static variable, so two threads calling it at the same time interfere with each other.
[x] `strtok_r` takes an extra argument in which the caller keeps the position, so each thread can keep its own state.
[ ] `strtok` is unsafe for threads mainly because it allocates memory.
[ ] A function that uses only its parameters and local variables still cannot be called safely by several threads at once, even on different data.
[ ] A static variable declared inside a function is private to each thread.
---
「用 static 變數記位置，兩條執行緒互相干擾」對：函式內的 static 變數只有一份，全行程共用。執行緒甲切到一半，執行緒乙的呼叫把位置換成自己的字串。

「`strtok_r` 讓呼叫者保管狀態」對：thread-safe 版本把狀態交給呼叫者。

「因為配置記憶體」錯：`strtok` 不配置記憶體，它直接在原字串裡寫入結束字元。

「只用參數和區域變數也不能同時呼叫」錯：區域變數在各自的堆疊上，不共享；對不同資料同時呼叫是安全的。

「函式內的 static 變數每條執行緒一份」錯：它跟全域變數一樣只有一份。

@q pr3-18 | os-process#3 | 2 | 計算
一個行程開 8 條執行緒，每條的堆疊預設 8 MB。光堆疊就佔多少虛擬位址空間？
== 64
== 64 MB
---
$8\times8$。每條執行緒有自己的堆疊（區域變數、呼叫紀錄），開很多執行緒時要注意這部分。

@q pr3-19 | os-process#3 | 3 | 證明
說明為什麼同一行程的執行緒可以共用 heap 和全域變數，卻一定要各自有自己的堆疊和暫存器。
---
堆疊存的是「目前執行到哪一層函式、區域變數是多少」，暫存器存的是「正在算的值和下一條指令」，這是每條執行緒各自的執行進度，共用的話兩條會互相蓋掉呼叫紀錄、回不到正確的地方。heap 和全域變數是資料，大家本來就要能看到，共用才方便合作（但要自己同步）。

@q pr4-06 | os-process#4 | 3 | 陷阱
On x86-64, user programs enter the kernel with the `syscall` instruction. Before executing it, the C library puts the system call number in `rax`, for example 1 for `write`, and the arguments in other registers. During boot, the kernel writes its system-call entry address into a model-specific register with the `wrmsr` instruction, and `syscall` then switches the CPU to kernel mode and jumps to that address. A student wonders whether `syscall` itself must be privileged, who chooses where it jumps, why `wrmsr` must be privileged, and why the program could not simply call the kernel's code with an ordinary `call` instruction instead. The student also wonders what would stop a malicious program from jumping straight into the middle of the kernel's code, past its checks, if it could choose the target. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] `syscall` is not a privileged instruction; user programs must be able to execute it.
[x] Executing `syscall` switches the CPU to kernel mode and jumps to an entry address that the operating system configured beforehand.
[ ] The user program chooses the kernel address that `syscall` jumps to.
[x] The instruction that sets that entry address is privileged.
[x] An ordinary function call into kernel code would not switch the CPU to kernel mode.
---
「`syscall` 不是特權指令」對：它就是給使用者程式進入核心用的；如果是特權指令，使用者程式就永遠請不到作業系統。

「切到核心模式、跳到作業系統事先設定的位址」對：入口固定，程式只能「敲門」，不能決定進哪個房間。

「程式自己選跳到哪」錯：那樣就能跳進核心任何地方，保護失效。

「設定入口位址的指令是特權指令」對：只有作業系統能設。

「一般函式呼叫不會切模式」對：而且核心的程式碼和資料在使用者模式下也不能存取，所以一定要靠 `syscall` 這種指令。

@q pr4-07 | os-process#4 | 2 | 情境
1 processor runs a user program whose machine instructions implement an endless loop with no voluntary calls into the kernel. A protected periodic timer is configured to interrupt every 10 ms; another runnable program needs 2 ms. The user program cannot disable the timer or mask its interrupt. Compare this preemptive design with a separate cooperative model having no other interrupt source. Assume the loop is actually present in the executable. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[x] A periodic timer interrupt returns control to the kernel, so other processes can still run.
[ ] Setting the timer can safely be left unprivileged, since the kernel can always interrupt the program anyway.
[ ] The CPU hardware detects infinite loops and stops them.
[ ] Even without a timer, a system that relies on programs giving up the CPU voluntarily would not be stuck by this program.
[ ] The compiler always removes such loops, so they never run.
---
「計時器定期中斷，讓核心拿回控制權」對：這是搶先式排程的基礎。

「設定計時器可以不是特權指令」錯：核心能打斷程式，靠的正是計時器；使用者若能設定它，就能把它關掉、霸占 CPU。

「CPU 會偵測無窮迴圈」錯：一般來說無法判斷一個程式會不會停。

「沒有計時器也不會卡住」錯：協作式系統要等程式自己讓出 CPU，這個迴圈永遠不讓；早期的協作式多工就有這個問題。

「編譯器一定會刪掉」錯：迴圈有可能被保留（例如等待某個外部事件的寫法），不能靠編譯器。

@q pr4-08 | os-process#4 | 2 | 觀念
In a two-mode CPU model, a user program at address 1000 attempts a privileged instruction that would disable interrupts. The protected hardware rule raises a trap before that instruction can change interrupt state, and the kernel handler begins at address 8000. The next sequential instruction is at 1004, but no silent-skip policy is provided. Compare detecting the forbidden attempt with an authorized system call; the kernel has not promised to perform the requested privileged action. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The CPU does not execute it; instead it traps and transfers control to the operating system.
[ ] The operating system usually performs the instruction on the program's behalf and lets it continue.
[ ] The instruction executes normally, because the program is allowed to choose its own mode.
[ ] The CPU silently skips the instruction and continues with the next one.
[x] The check of the current mode is done by the hardware on every such instruction.
---
「不執行，引發例外交給作業系統」對：這是雙模式保護的核心。

「作業系統代為執行再讓它繼續」錯：那等於使用者程式能間接關中斷；作業系統通常是終止程式，例如 Unix 上會送出非法指令的訊號。

「正常執行」錯：使用者程式不能自己決定模式。

「默默跳過」錯：那樣程式會以為成功了，錯誤會被隱藏。

「硬體檢查模式」對：軟體檢查來不及也不可靠，必須由 CPU 在執行時檢查模式位元。

@q pr4-09 | os-process#4 | 3 | 情境

@q pr4-10 | os-process#4 | 2 | 比較

On a 4-core machine, module 1 is loaded while existing applications continue; module 2 is held for later. Assume loading is permitted by the configuration and policy, and focus on where accepted module code executes. 「可載入的核心模組」對：需要時載入、不需要時卸載，保有單核心的效率又有一點彈性。

「核心服務在同一個位址空間」對：這就是單核心的定義，彼此直接呼叫，很快。

@q pr4-13 | os-process#4 | 2 | 情境
Compare a monolithic kernel with a microkernel model whose driver is a separate user process. The driver attempts CPU stores to addresses 1000,2000,3000, all outside its permitted user memory. In the microkernel trial there are no shared writable buffers with other services, and hardware protection rejects these stores; clients can tolerate a driver restart. [x] In a monolithic kernel, the driver runs in kernel space and can corrupt kernel data, possibly crashing the whole system.
[x] In a microkernel, the driver runs as a user-level process, so the damage is confined to that process, which can be restarted.

---
「單核心中可能拖垮系統」對：驅動和核心在同一個位址空間，沒有保護。

「微核心的保護沒有成本」錯：每次請求都要切換行程、以訊息經過核心，有額外的成本。

「微核心中仍能寫核心記憶體」錯：使用者模式下硬體不允許。

@q pr4-14 | os-process#4 | 3 | 陷阱

@q pr4-15 | os-process#4 | 3 | 情境
A link delivers 10000000000 packet bits per second, with each packet containing 12000 bits in the model. Ignore additional wire overhead and charge 2 microseconds of processor setup per packet interrupt, excluding useful packet-processing work. Compare the required setup time with one core's available second. Then consider packets of 72000 bits at the same data rate and a policy that batches packets per interrupt. Reducing interrupt setup does not eliminate useful processing work. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] About 830,000 packets arrive per second.
[x] Handling them would need about 1.67 s of processor time per second, more than one core can provide.
[x] Even though 2 μs is short, one interrupt per packet is too costly at this rate.
[x] Combining several packets per interrupt, or switching to polling under heavy load, reduces this overhead.
[x] Using packets of 72,000 bits instead would cut the rate to about 139,000 interrupts per second.
---
「約 83 萬個封包／秒」對：$10^{10}/12000\approx8.3\times10^5$。

「需要約 1.67 秒 CPU／秒」對：$8.3\times10^5\times2\,\mu\text{s}$。一顆核心光處理中斷就不夠用。

「2 μs 很短也不行」對：次數太多時，單次再短也會累積成瓶頸。

「合併中斷或改輪詢」對：Linux 的 NAPI 在負載高時改用輪詢，一次處理一批。

「72,000 位元的封包約 13.9 萬次」對：$10^{10}/72000\approx1.39\times10^5$，封包變大，中斷也變少。五句都對。

@q pr4-16 | os-process#4 | 2 | 比較
裝置事件用「中斷」和用「輪詢（polling）」各適合什麼情況？
[x] 事件少、間隔長用中斷（CPU 不用空等）；事件非常密集或要極低延遲時輪詢反而划算
[ ] 永遠用中斷
[ ] 永遠用輪詢
[ ] 中斷只能用在鍵盤
---
中斷每次有固定的切換成本；輪詢在沒事時浪費 CPU。兩者是取捨。

@q pr4-17 | os-process#4 | 1 | 計算
使用者程式呼叫 `write()` 把字串印到螢幕，CPU 的模式切換了幾次（使用者 → 核心算一次）？
== 2
---
使用者 → 核心（進入系統呼叫），核心 → 使用者（返回）。中間如果發生其他中斷會更多，這裡只算這次呼叫本身。

@q pr4-18 | os-process#4 | 2 | 證明
為什麼「把 CPU 切到核心模式」不能設計成一條普通指令讓使用者程式直接執行？
---
若使用者程式能自己切到核心模式，之後就能執行任何特權指令（關中斷、改記憶體保護、直接存取裝置），作業系統的保護全部失效。所以只能透過 trap／中斷這種「同時切模式並跳到核心指定入口」的機制進入，核心掌握進去之後做什麼。

@q pr5-06 | os-process#5 | 2 | 觀念
兩個行程用共享記憶體實作有限大小的緩衝區（一個放、一個拿）。除了共享記憶體本身，還一定需要什麼？
[x] 同步機制：避免同時改索引、緩衝區滿時讓放的人等、空時讓拿的人等
[ ] 不需要其他東西
[ ] 一條 pipe
[ ] 讓兩個行程變成同一個
---
共享記憶體只提供「看得到同一塊」，誰先誰後、互相等待要自己處理。訊息傳遞則由核心幫你排隊。

@q pr5-07 | os-process#5 | 1 | 計算
一般 pipe 是單向的。父子行程要互相傳資料（雙向），至少要幾條 pipe？
== 2
---
一條父寫子讀、一條子寫父讀。每個行程要把自己用不到的那端關掉。

@q pr5-08 | os-process#5 | 3 | 找錯
父行程建 pipe 後 fork，子行程寫完資料關掉寫端；父行程迴圈 `read` 讀到回傳 0（EOF）為止，但父行程**沒有關掉自己那份寫端**。結果？
[x] 父行程讀完資料後永遠卡在 `read`：還有寫端開著（就是它自己），核心不會回 EOF
[ ] 正常讀到 EOF
[ ] read 回傳 −1
[ ] 子行程會卡住
---
EOF 的條件是「所有寫端都關了」。pipe 程式最常見的 bug：fork 後忘了關用不到的那端。

@q pr5-09 | os-process#5 | 2 | 情境
shell 執行 `cat log | grep ERR | wc -l`，要建幾條 pipe、跑幾個行程（不含 shell）？
== 2,3
?? pipe,行程
---
三個指令各一個行程，相鄰兩個之間一條 pipe。三個行程同時在跑，資料像流水線一樣邊產生邊處理。

@q pr5-10 | os-process#5 | 2 | 比較
訊息傳遞的「直接」和「間接（mailbox）」方式？
[x] 直接要指名對方是誰；間接是送到信箱，誰來拿都可以，一個信箱可以多對多
[ ] 直接比較慢
[ ] 間接不需要核心
[ ] 兩者一樣
---
間接方式讓送的人和收的人不用知道彼此，例如工作佇列。

@q pr5-11 | os-process#5 | 2 | 觀念
訊息佇列容量為 0（沒有緩衝）時，送訊息的一方？
[x] 要等到接收方真的收下才能繼續（rendezvous，會合）
[ ] 馬上返回
[ ] 訊息被丟掉
[ ] 不能送
---
容量有限：滿了才等；容量無限：永遠不等。

@q pr5-12 | os-process#5 | 2 | 計算
透過核心傳一則訊息（送方寫進核心、收方從核心讀出），資料被複製幾次？用已經建好的共享記憶體呢？
== 2,0
?? 訊息傳遞,共享記憶體
---
使用者 → 核心、核心 → 使用者各一次；共享記憶體兩邊直接讀寫同一塊，不用經過核心複製。所以大量資料用共享記憶體快，但要自己同步。

@q pr5-13 | os-process#5 | 1 | 情境
兩台不同電腦上的服務要交換資料，該用哪一種？
[x] 訊息傳遞（例如網路 socket）
[ ] 共享記憶體
[ ] 一般 pipe
[ ] 全域變數
---
共享記憶體和一般 pipe 只能在同一台機器上；跨機器只能傳訊息。

@q pr5-14 | os-process#5 | 2 | 情境
瀏覽器的標籤頁行程不直接存取檔案或網路，而是傳訊息請主行程代勞。主要考量？
[x] 安全：標籤頁行程權限很低，就算被惡意網頁攻破，也只能透過受檢查的訊息要求事情
[ ] 速度比較快
[ ] 省記憶體
[ ] 程式比較好寫
---
訊息傳遞讓「誰能做什麼」集中在一個地方檢查，這就是沙盒。

@q pr5-15 | os-process#5 | 2 | 找錯
Through shared memory, process $P$ writes a 100-character record and its length, and process $Q$ repeatedly checks the stored length and reads the record as soon as the length is nonzero. No other synchronization is used. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[ ] $Q$ always sees the complete data before the nonzero length, because $P$ writes the data first.
[x] Synchronization or ordering guarantees are needed for $Q$ to read only complete records.
[ ] Shared memory automatically guarantees that writes become visible in program order to the other process.
[ ] Repeatedly checking the stored length costs no processor time while there is nothing to read.
[ ] $Q$ can safely read as soon as the length is nonzero, whatever order $P$ used for its writes.
---
「$Q$ 一定先看到完整資料」錯：$P$ 可能先寫長度；就算 $P$ 照順序寫，硬體也可能讓其他核心先看到長度，$Q$ 會讀到不完整的資料。

「需要正式的同步機制或順序保證」對：例如號誌、鎖，或在寫長度前加記憶體屏障。

「共享記憶體自動保證順序」錯：只保證兩邊看到同一塊記憶體，不保證時機和順序。

「一直檢查不花 CPU」錯：這是忙碌等待，沒東西讀時也一直佔著處理器；用會睡的同步機制比較好。

「不管順序都安全」錯：如上。

@q pr5-16 | os-process#5 | 3 | 陷阱
Two processes exchange messages with synchronous send (the sender waits until the receiver takes the message) and no buffering. Each process first sends to the other and then receives from the other. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Both processes wait forever, because each send waits for a receive that the other process will reach only after its own send.
[x] Letting one process send first while the other receives first avoids the problem.
[ ] The exchange completes, because the messages are short.
[x] With a buffer that can hold at least one message in each direction, both sends complete and the exchange works.
[ ] The kernel automatically reorders the two sends to avoid the problem.
---
「兩邊永遠等」對：沒有緩衝時，send 要等對方 receive；兩邊都卡在 send，形成循環等待。

「一邊先送、一邊先收」對：打破對稱。

「訊息短就沒事」錯：跟長度無關，跟有沒有緩衝有關。

「有緩衝就可以」對：send 把訊息放進緩衝區就回來，接著各自 receive。

「核心自動調整順序」錯：核心不會替程式改寫邏輯。

@q pr5-17 | os-process#5 | 3 | 比較
Which of the following statements about remote procedure calls (RPC), compared with ordinary local procedure calls, are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] After a request is sent, a network failure may leave the caller unable to tell whether the remote procedure was executed.
[x] Simply resending a request after a timeout is safe only if executing the operation twice has the same effect as executing it once.
[ ] Pointer arguments can be passed directly, since the remote procedure runs in the caller's address space.
[x] Arguments must be packed into a message in a format both machines agree on, since the two machines may represent data differently.
[ ] An RPC is usually faster than a local call because the server machine may be more powerful.
---
「不知道對方執行了沒」對：請求可能沒送到，也可能已經執行完、只是回覆丟了。這種「部分失敗」是本地呼叫沒有的。

「重送只對做兩次跟一次效果一樣的操作安全」對：例如「把餘額設成 500」可以重送，「餘額加 100」重送就可能加兩次。所以 RPC 系統要定義「最多一次／至少一次」的語意。

「指標可以直接傳」錯：遠端是另一個位址空間，指標在那邊沒有意義，得把指到的資料複製過去。

「參數要打包成雙方都認得的格式」對：兩台機器的整數排列方式（例如大小端）可能不同。

「RPC 通常比本地快」錯：多了打包、網路傳輸、拆包，延遲通常大上好幾個數量級。

@q pr5-18 | os-process#5 | 2 | 觀念
Which of the following statements about signals, such as the SIGINT sent by Ctrl+C, used for interprocess communication are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] A standard signal tells the receiver only which kind of event happened; it carries no data.
[x] The receiver can install a handler, for example to save its work before exiting on SIGINT.
[ ] Every signal terminates the receiving process.
[ ] Only the kernel can send a signal.
[x] SIGKILL cannot be caught or ignored by the receiver.
---
「只告訴哪一種事件」對：資訊量就是一個編號，要傳資料得用管道、共享記憶體等。

「可以自己處理」對：例如存檔後再結束。

「一定會結束」錯：很多訊號可以被處理或忽略。

「只有核心能送」錯：行程可以用 kill 送給另一個行程（要有權限）。

「SIGKILL 不能攔」對：保證系統一定能終止失控的行程。

@q pr5-19 | os-process#5 | 2 | 計算
A pipe has a 64 KB buffer. The writer calls write once with 100 KB in the default mode, where write waits whenever the buffer is full, and the reader has so far read 40 KB. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The write has finished and returned: after 40 KB were read, there was room for the remaining 36 KB.
[ ] The writer is still inside write.
[ ] The write returned an error after writing 64 KB.
[x] If the reader had read only 30 KB, the writer would still be inside write.
[x] When the buffer is full, the writer sleeps until the reader frees some space.
---
「已經寫完返回」對：$100-64=36\le40$。

「還卡在 write」錯。

「寫 64 KB 就回錯誤」錯：阻塞模式會等，不會因為滿了就回錯誤。

「只讀 30 KB 還卡著」對：$36>30$，還差 6 KB 放不下。

「滿了就睡，讀走才醒」對：寫端在緩衝滿時等待，讀端讀走後被叫醒繼續寫。

@q sc1-06 | os-sched#1 | 2 | 計算
行程 P 在時間 2 到達、需要 CPU 6 個單位，被切成兩段執行：時間 4–7 和 10–13。周轉、等待、回應時間各是？
== 11,5,2
?? 周轉,等待,回應
---
周轉＝完成 − 到達＝13−2；等待＝周轉 − 執行＝11−6（在 ready 佇列的時間 2–4、7–10 加起來也是 5）；回應＝第一次開始 − 到達＝4−2。被搶先過時，等待≠開始 − 到達。

@q sc1-07 | os-sched#1 | 2 | 計算
0 到 24 之間，CPU 在 0–5、7–13、15–20、22–24 有工作，其他時間閒著。CPU 使用率是幾 %？
== 75
---
忙 $5+6+5+2=18$，$18/24=75\%$。

@q sc1-08 | os-sched#1 | 3 | 陷阱
A process arrives at time 0 and needs 7 ms of execution in total. Its history, in milliseconds, is:
```
0-4    running on the CPU
4-10   waiting for a disk read to complete
10-12  in the ready queue, waiting for the CPU
12-15  running on the CPU, then it exits at 15
```
Is the time spent on the disk read counted in its waiting time as used for CPU scheduling? Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Its waiting time is 2 ms, because only the time in the ready queue counts.
[x] Its turnaround time of 15 ms equals waiting time plus execution time plus I/O time.
[ ] Its waiting time is 8 ms, because it was not running from time 4 to time 12.
[ ] Only the time after a process has been preempted counts as waiting time.
[ ] Whether the disk read counts as waiting time depends on which scheduling algorithm is used.
---
「等待 2 ms」對：排程法只能控制 ready 佇列的順序，磁碟 I/O 的時間它管不到，所以等待時間只算在 ready 佇列等 CPU 的 10～12。

「周轉 15＝2＋7＋6」對：有 I/O 時，周轉要把 I/O 時間也加進去。

「等待 8 ms」錯：把 4～10 的磁碟讀取也算進去了。

「只算被搶走之後的」錯：還沒開始前、或做完 I/O 後在 ready 佇列等的，都算等待時間。

「看用哪種排程法」錯：定義和用哪種排程法無關。

@q sc1-09 | os-sched#1 | 2 | 計算
三個行程執行時間 3、4、5，某個排程的平均等待時間是 5。平均周轉時間是？
== 9
---
（沒有 I/O 時）每個行程周轉＝等待＋執行，平均也一樣：$5+\frac{3+4+5}3=9$。

@q sc1-10 | os-sched#1 | 3 | 計算
A（到達 0、執行 6）、B（1、2）、C（2、1）。時間軸：A 0–3、B 3–5、A 5–8、C 8–9。平均等待時間？
== 10/3
== 3.33
---
完成時間 A 8、B 5、C 9；周轉 8、4、7；等待＝周轉 − 執行：2、2、6，平均 $\frac{10}3$。C 雖然最短卻等最久，這個排程不好。

@q sc1-11 | os-sched#1 | 2 | 情境
A drawing program handles each button press with about 5 ms of processing. At the same time, a background export job needs 20 s of processing. In the current system, a running job keeps the CPU until it finishes or blocks, so a button press that arrives while the export is running may wait up to 20 s. Users complain that the screen reacts long after a button is pressed, although all the work does get finished eventually. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Response time is the measure that should be improved first.
[x] If button-press work could take the CPU as soon as it arrives, its response time would drop to a few milliseconds.
[x] Allowing the system to take the CPU away from the running job helps, because a press need not wait for the export to finish.
[x] Dividing execution into shorter time slices helps responsiveness, at the cost of more context switches.
[x] Improving throughput alone may not fix the complaint.
---
「先改善回應時間」對：使用者在意的是按下去多久有反應。

「一到就能拿到 CPU，回應降到幾毫秒」對：只要等自己的 5 ms 處理。

「能把 CPU 從執行中的工作拿走」對：這就是搶先；按鍵不必等 20 秒的工作做完。

「時間片切短有幫助但切換變多」對：每個工作輪到的間隔變短，代價是 context switch 次數增加。

「只提高產出量不一定有用」對：產出量高不代表反應快。五句都對。

@q sc1-12 | os-sched#1 | 3 | 觀念
Three processes arrive at time 0 and do no I/O; their execution times are 6, 2, and 4 ms. Consider running them in the order 6, 2, 4 and in the order 2, 4, 6, each to completion. In the first order the average waiting time is $14/3$ ms and the average turnaround time is $26/3$ ms; in the second order they are $8/3$ ms and $20/3$ ms. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[x] For every order of these processes, the average turnaround time equals the average waiting time plus 4 ms.
[ ] The order 2, 4, 6 lowers the average waiting time but makes the average turnaround time larger than the order 6, 2, 4 does.
[ ] Minimizing average waiting time and minimizing average turnaround time coincide only when processes run in arrival order.
[ ] The two goals coincide only when there is a single process.
[ ] The order 2, 4, 6 also gives each process its smallest possible waiting time.
---
「平均周轉＝平均等待＋4」對：每個行程的周轉＝等待＋自己固定的執行時間，平均起來差 $(6+2+4)/3=4$，跟順序無關，所以兩個目標其實是同一件事。

「2、4、6 讓平均周轉變大」錯：$20/3<26/3$，兩個平均一起變小。

「只在照到達順序時一致」錯、「只在一個行程時一致」錯：上面的推導對任何順序、任何行程數都成立。

「每個行程都等最少」錯：執行 6 ms 的那個在第一種順序等 0，在第二種要等 6。平均最小不代表每個人都等最少。

@q sc1-13 | os-sched#1 | 2 | 找錯
Process $P$ arrives at time 3 and needs 5 ms of execution in total. It first runs from 7 to 9, is then preempted, runs again from 12 to 15, and completes at 15. A student computes its waiting time as "completion time − arrival time" $= 15 - 3 = 12$ ms. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] What the student computed is the turnaround time.
[x] The waiting time of $P$ is 7 ms.
[ ] The response time of $P$ is $9 - 3 = 6$ ms, because response time is measured until the first preemption.
[ ] What the student computed is the response time.
[ ] The student's formula gives the correct waiting time whenever the process is never preempted.
---
「學生算的是周轉時間」對：完成－到達。

「等待 7 ms」對：周轉 12 減執行 5；也可以直接數：開始前等 7－3＝4，被搶走後等 12－9＝3，共 7。

「回應 6 ms」錯：回應時間算到第一次拿到 CPU，是 7－3＝4 ms。

「學生算的是回應時間」錯：回應只算到第一次跑。

「不被搶時就對」錯：就算不被搶，完成－到達也包含了執行時間，不等於等待時間。

@q sc2-25 | os-sched#2 | 2 | 計算
RR 時間片 2，四個行程都在時間 0 到，依 P1、P2、P3、P4 排隊，執行時間 5、3、1、4。平均回應時間？
== 2.75
---
第一次拿到 CPU：P1 0、P2 2、P3 4、P4 5（P3 只跑 1）。$(0+2+4+5)/4$。程式模擬驗算。

@q sc1-15 | os-sched#1 | 2 | 陷阱
A monitoring tool reports that, over the last 60 s, the CPU was busy 100% of the time. A closer look shows that about 30% of that time went to context switches, about 50% went to one process that repeatedly checks a lock in a loop without doing anything else, and only about 20% went to useful work; only 3 jobs finished during the minute. Someone says: "CPU utilization is 100%, so the system is performing well." Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The claim is wrong here, partly because much of the time goes to context switches.
[x] A process that loops while checking a lock can keep the CPU at 100% without doing useful work.
[x] Utilization only says that the CPU was not idle.
[x] Throughput and response time can be poor even at 100% utilization.
[ ] Lower CPU utilization always means that the system is performing better.
---
「這裡說法錯，部分因為 context switch」對：使用率只說 CPU 沒閒著，沒說它在做有用的事；這裡三成花在切換。

「檢查鎖的迴圈也能讓 CPU 100%」對：這叫忙碌等待，占了一半時間。

「使用率只表示沒閒著」對。

「100% 時產出量和回應仍可能很差」對：一分鐘只做完 3 件。

「使用率低一定比較好」錯：CPU 閒著也是浪費。

@q sc1-16 | os-sched#1 | 3 | 情境
Small jobs arrive one every 10 ms, and each needs 2 ms of work. Processed one at a time, each job also needs 1 ms of setup, so at most about 333 jobs per second can be handled. A new design waits until 50 jobs have arrived and then processes them together with a single 1 ms setup, so one batch takes 101 ms and the system can handle about 495 jobs per second. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The first job of a batch now waits 490 ms before the batch even starts.
[x] In this design, throughput and response time pull against each other.
[ ] CPU utilization drops, since the CPU idles while each batch is being gathered.
[ ] Throughput drops, since each job waits longer before it starts.
[ ] Batching improves both throughput and the response time of every single job.
---
「第一個工作要等 490 ms」對：單一工作的回應時間變長，要等湊滿 50 個才開始，第一個工作要等後面 49 個陸續到達，$49\times10=490$ ms。

「產出量和回應時間互相拉扯」對：上限從約 333 個／秒提高到 $50/0.101\approx495$ 個／秒，代價是回應變慢。

「CPU 使用率下降」錯：省下的是重複的設定工作，同樣的工作花更少的 CPU；不是因為等待而閒置更多。

「產出量下降」錯：見上，產出量上限提高了。

「兩邊都變好」錯：回應時間是代價。

@q sc1-17 | os-sched#1 | 1 | 計算
行程在時間 4 到達、執行 3、時間 9 開始、時間 12 完成（中間沒被打斷）。等待時間？
== 5
---
沒被打斷時等待＝開始 − 到達＝9−4；也等於周轉 8 − 執行 3。

@q sc1-18 | os-sched#1 | 2 | 比較
Three jobs arrive at time 0, each needing 4 ms of processing and no I/O. Schedule X runs them one after another to completion. Schedule Y gives each job 1 ms in turn, cycling through the three jobs until all are done. As a result, X has average turnaround time 8 ms and average response time 4 ms, while Y has average turnaround time 11 ms and average response time 1 ms. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[x] Schedule Y suits a text editor better, and schedule X suits a batch system better.
[ ] Schedule X suits a text editor better, because its average turnaround time is shorter.
[ ] Schedule Y suits a batch system better, because its average response time is shorter.
[ ] Both suit a text editor equally well, since every job finishes eventually.
[ ] Neither can be judged without also knowing the CPU utilization.
---
X：完成時間 4、8、12，平均周轉 8；開始時間 0、4、8，平均回應 4。Y：完成時間 10、11、12，平均周轉 11；開始時間 0、1、2，平均回應 1。

「Y 給編輯器、X 給批次」對：互動程式要按鍵後馬上有反應，看回應時間，Y 的 1 遠好於 4；批次系統看整件事多久做完，選周轉較短的 X。

「X 給編輯器」錯、「Y 給批次」錯：把兩種需求對調了。

「兩者一樣適合編輯器」錯：反應快慢差很多。

「要知道 CPU 使用率」錯：兩種排法 CPU 都一直在忙，差別看這兩個數字就夠判斷。

@q sc1-19 | os-sched#1 | 3 | 計算
先到先跑（不搶先），A（到達 0、執行 4）、B（1、3）、C（6、2）。CPU 會不會閒置？平均周轉時間？
== 不會,13/3
== 不會,4.33
?? 閒置,平均周轉
---
A 0–4、B 4–7、C 7–9：C 在 6 到時 B 還沒做完，所以 CPU 一直有事做。周轉 A $4-0=4$、B $7-1=6$、C $9-6=3$，平均 $\frac{13}3\approx4.33$。如果 C 在 8 才到，7–8 就會閒置。

@q sc2-13 | os-sched#2 | 2 | 情境
一個長的 CPU 密集行程和很多短的 I/O 密集行程，在 FCFS 下會出現什麼現象？
[x] convoy：短行程都排在長行程後面，CPU 忙的時候磁碟閒、長行程做 I/O 時 CPU 又閒，兩邊利用率都差
[ ] 短行程會先跑完
[ ] 長行程餓死
[ ] 沒有問題
---
可搶先或 SJF 類的排程能讓短的 I/O 行程先跑完、趕快回去做 I/O，讓 CPU 和磁碟同時忙。

@q sc2-14 | os-sched#2 | 1 | 觀念
Three processes with no I/O are scheduled with round robin and a time quantum of 10 ms:

| process | arrival (ms) | run time (ms) |
|---|---|---|
| $P_1$ | 0 | 8 |
| $P_2$ | 1 | 4 |
| $P_3$ | 2 | 9 |

So the quantum is larger than the run time of every process. A student wonders how this round robin compares with other algorithms, and whether changing the arrival order would change the average waiting time; for the order shown, the waiting times are 0, 7, and 10 ms, an average of about 5.7 ms. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] It behaves like FCFS.
[x] Each process runs to completion as soon as it gets the CPU.
[x] No process is ever preempted by the timer.
[x] The average waiting time then depends on the arrival order.
[ ] It behaves like SJF, since short processes finish within one quantum.
---
每個行程「一拿到 CPU 就跑完」，「計時器從不換人」，就是依到達順序的「FCFS」，平均等待「取決於到達順序」。它不看執行時間長短，所以不是 SJF。

@q sc2-15 | os-sched#2 | 2 | 計算
SJF 用指數平均預測下一次要用多久的 CPU：$\tau_{n+1}=\alpha t_n+(1-\alpha)\tau_n$，$\alpha=0.5$、$\tau_0=10$。實際依序是 6、4、6。$\tau_3$？
== 6
---
$\tau_1=0.5\cdot6+0.5\cdot10=8$；$\tau_2=0.5\cdot4+0.5\cdot8=6$；$\tau_3=0.5\cdot6+0.5\cdot6=6$。

@q sc2-16 | os-sched#2 | 2 | 觀念
指數平均預測中 $\alpha=0$ 和 $\alpha=1$ 各代表什麼？
[x] $\alpha=0$：永遠用最初的猜測，不看實際；$\alpha=1$：只看上一次的實際值
[ ] 兩個都是只看上一次
[ ] $\alpha=0$ 只看上一次
[ ] 兩個都不能用
---
$\alpha$ 越大越相信最近的表現、反應越快；越小越平滑。

@q sc2-17 | os-sched#2 | 3 | 計算
RR 時間片 2，A、B、C 都在 0 到，依序排隊，執行 5、3、2。不同行程之間的切換（context switch）共幾次（不算開頭和結尾）？
== 5
---
時間軸 A 0–2、B 2–4、C 4–6、A 6–8、B 8–9、A 9–10。換手：A→B、B→C、C→A、A→B、B→A。程式模擬驗算。

@q sc2-18 | os-sched#2 | 3 | 計算
A（0,6）、B（1,3）、C（3,1）、D（4,4）（到達, 執行）。SRTF 的平均等待時間？
== 2.5
---
A 0–1，B 1 到（剩 3 < A 剩 5）搶先 1–4；C 4–5；D 5–9；A 9–14。等待 A $14-0-6=8$、B 0、C 1、D 1，平均 2.5。程式模擬驗算。

@q sc2-19 | os-sched#2 | 3 | 比較
接上題同一組，FCFS、不可搶先 SJF、SRTF、RR(q=2) 的平均等待時間由小到大？
[x] SRTF 2.5 < SJF 3.75 < FCFS 4.25 < RR(2) 5.25
[ ] FCFS < SJF < SRTF < RR
[ ] RR < SRTF < SJF < FCFS
[ ] SJF < SRTF < RR < FCFS
---
程式模擬四種。RR 的平均等待通常最差，但回應時間最好。

@q sc2-20 | os-sched#2 | 2 | 陷阱
A print server uses non-preemptive SJF. At 9:00 a 600-page job $J$ arrives while the printer is busy, and from then on a 1-page job arrives every 5 seconds all day; each 1-page job takes 10 seconds to print. Whenever the printer becomes free, the scheduler picks the shortest job in the queue. The office manager asks whether $J$ will ever be printed, and what could be changed in the scheduler so that every job is eventually printed. Can non-preemptive SJF starve a long process? Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Yes: as long as shorter jobs keep arriving, the long process is never chosen.
[x] Aging, which raises priority with waiting time, is the usual remedy.
[x] The same can happen under preemptive SRTF.
[ ] No: non-preemptive scheduling can never cause starvation.
[ ] Round robin starves long processes in the same way.
---
SJF 每次挑最短的，「短工作源源不絕時長工作一直排不到」，「搶先式的 SRTF 也一樣」；解法是「aging」。會不會飢餓和搶不搶先無關；RR 每個行程輪流拿到時間片，不會飢餓。

@q sc2-21 | os-sched#2 | 3 | 找錯
Two processes are scheduled with SRTF (shortest remaining time first):

| process | arrival | burst |
|---|---|---|
| $P_1$ | 0 | 7 |
| $P_2$ | 2 | 2 |

A student draws the timeline as $P_1$ from 0 to 7 and $P_2$ from 7 to 9, and computes the waiting times from it: 0 for $P_1$ and 5 for $P_2$. The grader marks the timeline wrong and asks the student to redo it and to explain which algorithm the drawn timeline actually follows, since it does not match SRTF at time 2. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] At time 2, $P_2$'s remaining time 2 is less than $P_1$'s remaining 5, so $P_2$ should preempt.
[x] The correct timeline is $P_1$ 0–2, $P_2$ 2–4, $P_1$ 4–9.
[x] The student's chart is non-preemptive SJF.
[x] With SRTF, $P_2$'s waiting time is 0.
[ ] With SRTF, $P_1$'s waiting time is 0, since it arrived first.
---
SRTF 新行程到達時要重新比較剩餘時間：時間 2 時 $P_2$ 剩 2、$P_1$ 剩 5，「換 $P_2$」，得到「0–2、2–4、4–9」，「$P_2$ 一到就跑，等待 0」。學生畫的是「不可搶先 SJF」。$P_1$ 在 2–4 被換下來等了 2。

@q sc2-22 | os-sched#2 | 2 | 計算
最高回應比優先（HRRN）：回應比＝(已等待＋執行時間)／執行時間。某時刻 A 等了 8、要 4；B 等了 5、要 1；C 等了 9、要 9。選誰？
== B
---
A $12/4=3$、B $6/1=6$、C $18/9=2$。短工作比值高，但長工作等越久比值也會一直升，不會飢餓。

@q sc2-23 | os-sched#2 | 3 | 計算
三個行程都在 0 到，各需要 10。RR 時間片 1 和時間片 10 的平均周轉時間各是？（切換時間不計）
== 29,20
?? q=1,q=10
---
q=1：輪流跑，三個在 28、29、30 完成，平均 29；q=10：就是 FCFS，10、20、30，平均 20。時間片小不一定讓周轉變好，執行時間相近時反而最差。

@q sc2-24 | os-sched#2 | 2 | 計算
A（0,6）、B（1,3）、C（3,1）、D（4,4），RR 時間片 3（新到的排在被換下的前面）。平均等待時間？
== 3.75
---
A 0–3、B 3–6、C 6–7、A 7–10、D 10–14。等待 A 4、B 2、C 3、D 6，平均 3.75。程式模擬驗算。

@q sc3-08 | os-sched#3 | 1 | 計算
RM 的充分條件 $U\le n(2^{1/n}-1)$，$n=2$ 時上限約是多少？
== 0.828
---
$2(\sqrt2-1)\approx0.828$。$n\to\infty$ 時趨近 $\ln2\approx0.693$。

@q sc3-09 | os-sched#3 | 3 | 陷阱
Two periodic tasks have (period, execution time) $= (4, 2)$ and $(8, 4)$, deadlines equal to periods, and are released together at time 0, so the total utilization is $U = 2/4 + 4/8 = 1$, above the rate-monotonic (RM) bound of about 0.828 for 2 tasks. A student expects RM to miss a deadline and simulates it from time 0 to time 8, the least common multiple of the periods, giving the task with period 4 the higher priority, and writes down which task runs in each of the 8 time units. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[x] RM meets all deadlines for this task set.
[ ] RM must miss a deadline, because $U$ exceeds the RM bound.
[ ] The task with period 8 misses its first deadline at time 8.
[ ] Only EDF can meet all deadlines when $U=1$.
[ ] RM succeeds here only because both execution times are even numbers.
---
8 是 4 的 2 倍，週期成倍數時 RM 可以用到 100%：0–2 第一個、2–4 第二個、4–6 第一個、6–8 第二個，時間 8 剛好做完 4 單位，「全部趕上」。上限只是充分條件，超過不代表排不完；成功的原因是週期成倍數，和執行時間的奇偶無關。

@q sc3-10 | os-sched#3 | 1 | 計算
兩個工作 (2,1)、(3,2)。EDF 排得完嗎？
== 不能
---
$U=\frac12+\frac23=\frac76>1$：CPU 根本不夠，任何排程都排不完。

@q sc3-11 | os-sched#3 | 2 | 追蹤
RM，(週期 4, 執行 1)、(週期 6, 執行 3)。第二個工作的第一份在什麼時間完成？
== 4
---
時間 0 兩個都到，週期 4 的優先：0–1 跑 T1；1–4 跑 T2（3 個單位）完成在 4；時間 4 T1 第二份到。程式模擬驗算。

@q sc3-13 | os-sched#3 | 1 | 比較
即時系統分「硬即時」和「軟即時」，兩者的差別是？
[x] 硬即時錯過期限就算失敗（例如煞車控制）；軟即時錯過只是品質變差（例如影片掉格）
[ ] 硬即時比較快
[ ] 軟即時沒有期限
[ ] 兩者一樣
---
硬即時要能事先證明一定排得完，所以用 RM／EDF 這種可以分析的排程。

@q sc3-14 | os-sched#3 | 2 | 計算
週期 4、6、10 的三個工作，模擬驗證要跑多長（超週期）？
== 60
---
最小公倍數 $\text{lcm}(4,6,10)=60$，之後排法會重複。

@q sc3-15 | os-sched#3 | 3 | 計算
RM 下已有 (4,1)、(6,2)，要再加 (8,$x$)。用上限 $3(2^{1/3}-1)\approx0.780$ 只能保證 $x\le1$。實際模擬，RM 最大可以到多少（整數）？
== 2
---
上限法：$0.25+0.333+\frac x8\le0.780$ ⇒ $x\le1.57$。實際 $x=2$（$U\approx0.83$）模擬一個超週期沒錯過；$x=3$ 時週期 8 的工作錯過期限（EDF 還排得完）。充分條件很保守。

@q sc3-16 | os-sched#3 | 2 | 找錯
A student implements rate-monotonic (RM) scheduling for 3 periodic tasks with (period, execution time) $(10, 4)$, $(20, 2)$, and $(40, 1)$ by giving the higher priority to the task with the shorter execution time, reasoning that short jobs finish sooner. The teaching assistant points out that the resulting priority order is exactly the reverse of the one RM would use for these tasks, and asks the student to restate how RM assigns priorities and whether they ever change at run time, for example when a job is close to its deadline. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] RM priorities depend on the period: a shorter period gets a higher priority.
[x] RM priorities are static and fixed in advance.
[ ] The student's rule is RM, since short jobs finish sooner.
[ ] RM gives the nearest deadline the highest priority at each moment.
[ ] RM gives higher priority to tasks with longer execution times.
---
RM「看週期，越短越優先」，而且「事先排好不變」。執行時間長短都不是 RM 的依據；「期限越近越優先」是 EDF，每份工作動態決定。

@q sc3-17 | os-sched#3 | 3 | 比較
Earliest-deadline-first (EDF) scheduling can meet all deadlines of any periodic task set with utilization up to $U = 1$, while rate-monotonic (RM) scheduling is guaranteed only up to about 0.69 for many tasks. Nevertheless, a car manufacturer's engine controller, with 25 periodic tasks, uses RM with fixed priorities on a kernel that offers 32 priority levels. A new engineer asks why the company does not switch to EDF, and a senior engineer lists several practical reasons, including what happens during a short overload. Which of the following statements explain why many real systems still use RM (fixed priorities)? Select all that apply.
[x] RM is simple to implement and its behavior is predictable.
[x] Under overload, EDF may let a cascade of jobs miss deadlines, while RM still protects the short-period tasks.
[x] EDF must compute priorities dynamically for every job.
[x] Fixed priorities match the priority levels that most real-time kernels provide.
[ ] RM does not need to know the task periods.
---
RM 用固定優先權，「簡單、可預測」，「直接對應到即時核心的優先權層級」；EDF「每份工作要動態算」，「超載時可能連鎖錯過期限」，RM 至少保住短週期的工作。RM 正是依週期決定優先權，一定要知道週期。

@q sc3-18 | os-sched#3 | 2 | 情境
汽車的煞車控制每 10 ms 要跑 2 ms，安全氣囊感測每 5 ms 要跑 1 ms，儀表板更新每 50 ms 要跑 10 ms。CPU 使用率？用 RM 上限（$n=3$ 約 0.78）能保證排得完嗎？
== 0.6,能
?? U,能否保證
---
$\frac2{10}+\frac15+\frac{10}{50}=0.6\le0.78$，RM 保證排得完。

@q sc3-19 | os-sched#3 | 3 | 證明
說明為什麼 $U>1$ 時任何排程法都排不完週期性工作。
---
取一個超週期 $H$（所有週期的公倍數），這段時間內各工作要做的總量是 $\sum\frac H{p_i}c_i=HU>H$，但 CPU 在長度 $H$ 的時間裡最多只能做 $H$ 單位的工作，一定有工作做不完、錯過期限。

@q sc4-06 | os-sched#4 | 2 | 追蹤
MLFQ 三層：第 0 層時間片 8、第 1 層 16、第 2 層 FCFS。一個 CPU 密集工作需要 30，沒有其他工作。它在三層各跑多少？
== 8,16,6
---
用完時間片就降一層：8（降）→ 16（降）→ 剩 6 在最底層跑完。

@q sc4-07 | os-sched#4 | 2 | 觀念
In a basic MLFQ trial, a job at level 1 has an 8-ms slice, executes for 3 ms, and blocks for I/O. The specified rule retains its current level after an early block and starts a fresh slice when it becomes runnable. There is no priority boost, cumulative-use demotion or other rule in this trial. Compare queue membership while blocked with queue level upon return; temporary waiting does not mean permanent removal. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[x] When it returns, it stays at its current level.
[ ] It drops one level, since it gave up the CPU early.
[ ] It drops to the lowest level, since I/O-bound jobs get low priority.
[ ] It moves up one level every time it does I/O.
[ ] It is removed from the queues permanently.
---
基本規則下，沒用完時間片代表它像互動工作，「留在原本那一層」。用完時間片才降級；做 I/O 不會讓它升級，也不會被移除。（為了防止有人故意在快用完時做 I/O，進階版會累計用掉的 CPU 才決定降級。）

@q sc4-08 | os-sched#4 | 3 | 情境
A basic MLFQ top level uses a 10-ms slice and forgets earlier use after every I/O return. A program repeatedly executes 9 ms and performs 1 ms of I/O, avoiding slice exhaustion under that rule. Compare this behavior with a modified policy that retains cumulative execution at each level and demotes after a 10-ms allotment. The modified policy can measure execution without identifying the program's motives. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The fix is to account CPU use cumulatively at each level and demote the program once its allotment is used up.
[x] This trick is a classic way of gaming the scheduler.
[ ] The fix is to forbid programs in the top level from doing any I/O at all.
[ ] The fix is to make the time slices longer, so the trick no longer fits in a slice.
[ ] It cannot be prevented, since the scheduler cannot see why a program does I/O.
---
「在每一層累計用掉的 CPU」，超過配額就降級，不管中間有沒有 I/O，這招就沒用；這是「經典的騙排程器手法」。禁止 I/O 不可能；時間片加長它還是在快用完時做 I/O；不必知道動機，看累計用量就能防。

@q sc4-09 | os-sched#4 | 2 | 觀念
MLFQ 為什麼要定期把所有工作「重新拉回最高層」（priority boost）？
[x] 避免底層的長工作餓死，也讓行為改變（從計算變互動）的工作有機會回到高層
[ ] 讓高層的工作跑更久
[ ] 減少 context switch
[ ] 讓 CPU 使用率變 100%
---
不 boost 的話，只要一直有互動工作，底層就永遠排不到。

@q sc4-10 | os-sched#4 | 2 | 計算
CFS 中，權重 1024 和 2048 的兩個行程各實際跑了 10 ms。各自的 vruntime 增加多少（權重 1024 當作 1 倍）？
== 10,5
---
vruntime 增量＝實際時間 × 1024／權重。權重大的增加得慢，所以會被挑得比較頻繁，分到比較多 CPU。

@q sc4-11 | os-sched#4 | 3 | 陷阱
Consider a simplified classic CFS placement policy whose current runnable vruntimes are 1000,1005,1010. Newly created and long-sleeping tasks are placed near the current minimum, rather than receiving an unlimited historical claim. Compare admitting a new task at vruntime 0 with placing it near 1000, using smallest-vruntime selection and equal weights. The claims below concern this placement model; they do not prescribe exact constants for every kernel release. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] It is set near the smallest vruntime currently in the run queue, not to 0.
[x] Setting it to 0 would let the new process monopolize the CPU for a long time.
[x] A process waking from a long sleep is also adjusted, so it cannot save up a claim on the CPU.
[ ] It is set to 0, so that every new process starts on an equal footing.
[ ] It is set to the largest vruntime in the queue, so that it cannot jump ahead of anyone.
---
設成 0 的話新行程比所有人小很多，「會霸佔 CPU 好一陣子」，所以「設成接近目前最小的」；「睡很久醒來的也會調整」，不能存 CPU 時間。0 反而不公平；設成最大會讓新行程等很久。

@q sc4-12 | os-sched#4 | 3 | 計算
CFS 中 nice 0 的權重 1024、nice 5 的權重 335。兩個都一直要 CPU，nice 0 的那個長期分到約幾 % 的 CPU？
== 75
== 75.3
---
$\frac{1024}{1024+335}\approx75.3\%$。nice 每差 1 權重約差 1.25 倍，差 5 約 3 倍。

@q sc4-13 | os-sched#4 | 2 | 觀念
A classic CFS-style run queue stores 1024 waiting tasks in a height-balanced search tree ordered by vruntime. Their smallest, next-smallest and largest values are 10,12,5000. A separate pointer always identifies the leftmost node, and insertions and deletions update it as needed. Distinguish selecting the next node through that pointer from updating the ordered structure after a task starts or stops waiting. Balance gives worst-case height guarantees regardless of arrival order. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The process with the smallest vruntime can be found quickly.
[x] Insertion and deletion take $O(\log n)$ time.
[x] Caching the leftmost node makes choosing the next process $O(1)$.
[x] The tree stays balanced, so these bounds hold in the worst case.
[x] The leftmost node holds the process with the smallest vruntime.
---
每次要挑 vruntime 最小的，就是「最左邊的節點」，「快取起來 $O(1)$」；平衡樹「插入刪除 $O(\log n)$」，「保持平衡所以是最壞情況」。五句都對。

@q sc4-14 | os-sched#4 | 3 | 計算
CFS 的目標延遲 20 ms（每個可執行的行程在這段時間內至少跑一次），最小時間片 4 ms。4 個權重相同的行程時每個分到幾 ms？10 個時呢？
== 5,4
?? 4 個,10 個
---
4 個：$20/4=5$。10 個：$20/10=2<4$，改用最小時間片 4，週期拉長成 40 ms。太多行程時要防止切換太頻繁。

@q sc4-15 | os-sched#4 | 2 | 比較
A four-core scheduler has runnable queue lengths 6,2,0,0. A periodic balancing routine moves work from a busy core to an idle core, while a separate idle-core routine may request work from another queue. Classify these actions as push or pull by which side initiates them. The routines may coexist, and neither mechanism depends on having only one processor. Compare triggers separately from the eventual destination of a migrated job. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Push migration may be initiated by a periodic routine moving work from busy cores.
[x] Pull migration is initiated when an idle core takes work from another queue.
[x] Both mechanisms may coexist in one multiprocessor scheduler.
[ ] Pull migration requires a single-core machine with no other processor to supply work.
[ ] The two names specify identical triggers and leave no distinction in who initiates the move.
---
「週期程式推走工作」對。「閒核心主動拉」對。「可共存」對：處理不同觸發情境。「單核才可拉」錯：題設需要從其他核心取工作。「觸發完全相同」錯：發起者不同，不能只看結果都有工作移動。

@q sc4-16 | os-sched#4 | 2 | 情境
記憶體分散在各處理器旁邊（用別的處理器旁的記憶體比較慢）的機器上，作業系統盡量讓行程留在原本的 CPU、用靠近它的記憶體。理由？

[ ] 換 CPU 會出錯
[ ] 為了公平
[ ] 為了省電

這就是處理器親和性；代價是負載可能不平均。

@q sc4-17 | os-sched#4 | 1 | 比較
Compare two four-processor operating-system models. In the symmetric model, each processor may schedule its own work with equal authority. In the asymmetric model, one designated master performs scheduling and shared kernel-data operations while the other 3 execute delegated work. Assume the master can handle 1000 requests per second but combined demand reaches 1200. Evaluate simplicity and the possible master bottleneck under these definitions, without treating this model as a survey of all current operating systems. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The specified symmetric model gives processors equal scheduling status.
[x] The specified asymmetric model centralizes scheduling and system work on its master.
[x] Centralizing the specified kernel-data operations simplifies concurrent access to those data.
[x] The master may be a bottleneck when incoming requests exceed its service rate.
[ ] In the specified asymmetric model, all four processors independently update the shared kernel data with equal authority.
---
「對稱各自排程」對。「非對稱集中主處理器」對。「集中存取較簡單」對：此題其他處理器不直接同時動該資料。「主處理器成瓶頸」對：1200大於每秒1000的服務率。「四顆都有同等修改權」錯：直接違反此處非對稱模型的工作分配。

@q sc4-18 | os-sched#4 | 2 | 找錯
Use a basic feedback scheduler with high, middle and low levels, giving slices of 4,8,16 ms. A new job starts high, full-slice use causes demotion, and an early I/O block retains the current level. Job R runs for 20 ms without an early I/O wait; job S repeatedly uses 1 ms before waiting. Analyze a continued stream of new high-level jobs, both with and without periodic boosts that return waiting jobs to the top. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] MLFQ does not need to know in advance how long each job will use the CPU: a job that uses up its whole time slice is moved to a lower-priority level.
[x] A job that gives up the CPU before its time slice ends, for example to wait for I/O, tends to stay at a high-priority level.
[ ] MLFQ can only place a job at the correct level if it is known before the job starts how long the job will use the CPU.
[x] Without periodically moving jobs back to the top level, long jobs at the lowest level may never get to run.
[ ] Unlike MLFQ, SJF needs no information about how long jobs will run.
---
「不用事先知道，用完時間片就降級」對：MLFQ 是從工作過去的行為**學出來**的。

「時間片還沒用完就交出 CPU 的留在高層」對：互動式、I/O 多的工作因此反應快。

「要事先知道才能放對層」錯：MLFQ 看工作實際怎麼用 CPU 來調整層級，用完時間片就往下降，不需要事先知道。

「不定期往上提，底層長工作可能永遠輪不到」對：高層一直有新工作時會餓死底層，所以會定期把所有工作提回最高層。

「SJF 不需要長度資訊」錯：剛好相反，SJF 要預知（或預測）下一次 CPU 使用時間，這正是它不實際、MLFQ 比較實用的地方。

@q sc4-19 | os-sched#4 | 3 | 情境
A machine has 4 identical physical cores, each exposing 2 hardware threads. Two independent compute-bound jobs share no data, and each alone saturates one core's execution units for 10 ms. In the specified throughput model, two threads sharing one core divide those same execution resources, while separate cores supply independent resources. Ignore memory delays and migration overhead for this trial. Use physical-core topology when comparing placements among the 8 logical CPUs. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The scheduler should place them on two different physical cores.
[x] The OS must know which logical CPUs belong to the same physical core.
[ ] Placing both on the two hardware threads of one core is best, since they then share a cache.
[ ] Any two logical CPUs give the same performance, since all 8 are equivalent.
[ ] Hyper-threading doubles each core's execution units, so sharing a core costs nothing.
---
同一核心的兩個硬體執行緒共用運算單元，擠在一起會互相拖慢，所以「放在兩個不同的實體核心」，作業系統「要知道拓撲」才能這樣判斷。共用快取的好處抵不過搶運算單元；8 個邏輯 CPU 並不等價；hyper-threading 沒有把運算單元加倍。

@q sy1-08 | os-sync#1 | 3 | 計算
兩條執行緒各做 2 次 `count++`（每次是「讀進暫存器」「加 1 寫回」兩步，可以任意交錯），count 從 0 開始。最後可能的值有哪些？
== 2 3 4
== 2,3,4
---
程式窮舉所有交錯：最好 4；最壞是 2（例如 A 讀 0 後被打斷，B 做完兩次寫成 2，A 寫回 1；B 再讀 1… 等交錯可以只剩 2）。所以「至少 2」而不是「至少 1」。

@q sy1-09 | os-sync#1 | 2 | 計算
count 原本 5，一條執行緒做 `count++`、另一條做 `count--`（都是讀、改、寫），同時跑。最後可能是？
== 4 5 6
== 4,5,6
---
正確答案應該是 5；但兩條都先讀到 5 時，後寫的會蓋掉先寫的，結果是 4 或 6。程式窮舉驗證只有這三種。

@q sy1-10 | os-sync#1 | 2 | 觀念
「競爭條件」（race condition）的定義是？
[x] 多個執行者存取共享資料，結果取決於它們執行的先後交錯
[ ] 兩個行程同時要 CPU
[ ] 程式跑太快
[ ] 兩個行程同時印東西
---
重點是「結果不確定、看運氣」。只讀不寫的共享資料不會有競爭條件。

@q sy1-11 | os-sync#1 | 3 | 計算

@q sy1-12 | os-sync#1 | 2 | 找錯
A student implements a lock for a multithreaded program with an ordinary shared variable `lock`, initially 0:
```
enter():  while (lock == 1) ;  lock = 1;
leave():  lock = 0;
```
In a test with 4 threads, each entering the critical section $10^5$ times, the shared counter protected by this lock occasionally ends up too small. The student is surprised, because each thread checks the lock before setting it, and wonders whether the problem also appears on a single-core machine, and what kind of hardware support would fix it on both kinds of machines. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] It violates mutual exclusion: two threads may both see lock == 0 before either sets it to 1.
[x] The check and the set are two separate steps that can be interrupted in between.
[ ] It violates only bounded waiting, since a thread that checks the lock may be overtaken many times.
[x] Doing the check and the set as one indivisible hardware operation fixes mutual exclusion.
[x] It also fails on a single-core machine, if a thread is switched out between the check and the set.
---
「違反互斥」對：兩個都通過檢查才設 1。

「檢查和設定是兩步」對：所以需要硬體提供一步完成的 test_and_set。

「只違反 bounded waiting」錯：連互斥都不成立。

「檢查和設定一步完成就互斥」對：例如 test_and_set。

「單核上也會錯」對：檢查完、設定前被換走，另一條一樣能進去。

@q sy1-13 | os-sync#1 | 2 | 比較
An operating-systems exam asks students to state the three requirements for a solution to the critical-section problem, and to classify a few example solutions. In one example, 2 processes must strictly take turns using a shared `turn` variable; in another, a process may have to wait for up to 3 turns of the other process but never longer. A grader of a class of 120 students collects the most common answers, some correct and some not, to discuss them in class next week. Which of the following statements about the three requirements for a critical-section solution are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Mutual exclusion: no two processes are in their critical sections at the same time.
[x] Progress: if no process is in its critical section, the choice of which waiting process enters next cannot be postponed forever.
[ ] Bounded waiting: after a process asks to enter, it must get in within a fixed number of milliseconds, however long others stay inside.
[ ] A solution where two processes must strictly take turns, using only a turn variable, satisfies progress.
[ ] Bounded waiting is what prevents two processes from entering at the same time.
---
「互斥：不同時進」對。

「progress：沒人在裡面時不能一直拖」對：防「大家都不進」。

「bounded waiting：固定毫秒內進去」錯：限制的是**別人插隊的次數**，不是時間；別人在臨界區裡待多久無法保證。它防的是「某人一直進不去」（餓死）。

「嚴格輪流滿足 progress」錯：輪到對方而對方不想進時，你也進不去。

「bounded waiting 防同時進」錯：那是互斥。

@q sy1-14 | os-sync#1 | 2 | 情境
An online booking system has 1 seat left on a flight. Two customers press "buy" at the same moment, and each request is handled by its own thread, which runs:
```
n = seats_left;          /* read */
if (n > 0) {
    seats_left = n - 1;  /* write */
    issue_ticket();
}
```
In a load test with 1000 such pairs of simultaneous requests, the system issues 2 tickets for the last seat in 37 cases and 1 ticket in all the others, and it never issues 0 tickets. One developer proposes making only the subtraction indivisible, and another argues that each request already checks the count before subtracting, so overselling should be impossible. The team must explain the 37 bad cases before the system goes live next week for all routes, and they suspect that both threads sometimes read `seats_left` before either one writes it back. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[ ] At most one ticket can be issued, since each request checks the count before subtracting.
[ ] Exactly one customer always gets the ticket.
[ ] Making only the subtraction indivisible is enough to prevent overselling.
[ ] Both customers always fail.
[x] This is a race condition: the result depends on how the two requests interleave.
---
「最多賣一張」錯：兩個請求都可能在對方扣除前讀到 1，都通過檢查，賣出兩張。

「一定只有一人拿到」錯：要看交錯。

「只讓扣除不可分割就夠」錯：問題出在「讀、查、扣」之間被插隊；三步要一起不可分割，例如加鎖或用資料庫交易。

「兩人都買不到」錯：至少先讀的那個會成功。

「這是競爭條件」對。

@q sy1-15 | os-sync#1 | 3 | 陷阱
Someone says: "My critical section is only one line of C, so it needs no protection," and points to the statement `count++;` in a program with 2 threads. On the target machine, the compiler translates this line into 3 instructions, a load, an add, and a store, and the operating system may switch threads after any instruction. The program runs both on a 4-core server and on a single-core embedded board, and the counter starts at 5 in a test where each thread runs the line exactly once. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] One line of source code, such as `count++`, may compile into several machine instructions, for example a load, an add, and a store.
[x] The CPU may switch to another thread between those machine instructions, and a thread on another core may run in between them.
[x] Even on a single-core machine, a one-line statement may need protection.
[x] If `count` is 5 and two threads each execute `count++` once, the final value can be 6.
[x] Short critical sections can still cause race conditions.
---
能不能被打斷看的是**機器指令**，不是原始碼行數。

「一行可能是好幾條機器指令」對：`count++` 是讀、加、寫三步。

「中斷或別的核心可以插進來」對：多核心時兩條指令序列真的同時跑；單核時中斷可能在任兩條指令之間發生，接著換別的執行緒跑。

「單核也可能要保護」對：如上，單核也會在讀和寫之間被切換。

「5 加兩次可能是 6」對：兩條都讀到 5、各自加成 6 再寫回。

「短的也會有競爭條件」對：時間片可能剛好在中間用完；而且多核心時根本不需要切換就會交錯。五句都對。

@q sy1-16 | os-sync#1 | 2 | 計算
兩條執行緒輪流（嚴格交替，用 `turn`）進入臨界區。執行緒 A 進去一次後再也不想進了，B 想進 3 次。B 能完成幾次？
== 1
---
A 出來把 turn 給 B，B 進 1 次後把 turn 給 A；A 不再進入，turn 永遠是 A，B 卡住。這就是違反 progress。

@q sy3-18 | os-sync#3 | 3 | 證明
說明「關掉中斷再進臨界區」為什麼在單處理器上能保證互斥。
---
單處理器上，執行中的程式只會因為中斷（計時器或裝置）被換下。關掉中斷後，在打開之前不會發生 context switch，也沒有其他處理器同時執行，所以臨界區內的指令不會和別的執行者交錯。代價是期間裝置事件延遲，所以臨界區要很短，而且只有核心能做。

@q sy1-18 | os-sync#1 | 2 | 觀念
A program with 8 threads shares several variables: a configuration table that the main thread fills in before starting any other thread and that nobody writes afterward, a counter that every thread increments about $10^4$ times, and a status flag that one thread writes while the others read it about 1000 times per second. A reviewer must decide which of these accesses need locks or other synchronization, and a junior developer proposes locking every shared variable on every read, just to be safe, even though this slows the program down. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] If no thread ever writes the variable, the threads can read it at the same time without a lock.
[x] If even one thread may write the variable while others read it, some synchronization is needed.
[ ] Reading a shared variable always requires a lock, even when nobody writes it.
[ ] A variable that is set once before any of the threads start must still be read under a lock, since it is shared.
[ ] Two threads reading the same variable at the same moment can corrupt its value.
---
「沒人寫就不用鎖」對：沒有人改，不管怎麼交錯讀到的都一樣。

「只要有人寫就要同步」對：讀到寫到一半的值，或讀到舊值後被別人改掉，都可能出錯。

「讀一定要鎖」錯：如上。

「啟動前設好的也要鎖」錯：之後沒人改，就是唯讀共享資料，例如設定值，直接讀即可。

「同時讀會損壞」錯：讀不會改變記憶體內容。

@q sy1-19 | os-sync#1 | 1 | 計算
