== 1 2
== 1,2
---
不交錯是 2；兩條都先讀 0 再各寫 1 就是 1。

@q sy2-06 | os-sync#2 | 3 | 陷阱
Someone swaps the first two statements of Peterson's entry section, so process $P_i$ runs turn = j; flag[i] = true; while (flag[j] && turn == j) wait;. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[ ] The swapped version still guarantees mutual exclusion, because flag[i] is set before the while loop checks anything.
[x] In the swapped version, both processes can end up in their critical sections at the same time.
[ ] The original order, flag[i] = true before turn = j, can also let both processes in at the same time.
[ ] In the swapped version, neither process can ever enter.
[ ] In the original order, when both want to enter, the process that writes turn first is the one that waits.
---
「對調後還互斥」錯、「對調後兩個都進得去」對：交錯反例：$P_0$ 寫 turn = 1；$P_1$ 寫 turn = 0、flag[1] = true，看到 flag[0] 還是 false 就進去；$P_0$ 接著寫 flag[0] = true，看到 turn = 0，也進去了。（程式窮舉兩個行程所有交錯可以找到。）

「原本的順序也會一起進」錯：先舉旗再讓步是必要的順序，原本的寫法保證互斥。

「對調後都進不去」錯：問題是兩個都進得去。

「先寫 turn 的那個等」錯：是**後**寫 turn 的那個等，因為 turn 最後的值讓給對方。

@q sy2-07 | os-sync#2 | 2 | 追蹤
Peterson：P0 想進、P1 不想進（flag[1] = false）。P0 執行 `flag[0]=true; turn=1;` 之後，while 條件 `flag[1] && turn==1` 是？P0 會等嗎？
[x] 假（flag[1] 是 false），P0 直接進入
[ ] 真，P0 等待
[ ] 假，但 P0 還是要等 P1
[ ] 無法判斷
---
對方不想進就不用等：這就是 progress。

@q sy2-08 | os-sync#2 | 2 | 觀念
Peterson 中 `turn` 變數的作用？
[x] 兩個都想進時決定誰讓誰：最後寫 turn 的那個讓步
[ ] 記錄誰在臨界區裡
[ ] 計算進入次數
[ ] 沒有作用，可以刪掉
---
只用 flag 會在兩人都舉旗時互等（死結）；只用 turn 會嚴格交替（違反 progress）。兩個合起來剛好三條件都滿足。

@q sy2-09 | os-sync#2 | 3 | 找錯
Two processes try to use only flags for mutual exclusion. Process $P_i$ (with $j = 1 - i$) runs:
`flag[i] = true; while (flag[j]) ;  /* critical section */  flag[i] = false;`
Both flags are initially false. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The two processes can never be in their critical sections at the same time.
[x] If both processes execute their first statement before either reaches the `while` loop, both wait forever.
[ ] Swapping the first two statements, so that each process waits for `flag[j]` to become false before setting `flag[i]`, fixes the problem and still guarantees mutual exclusion.
[x] Peterson's solution adds the shared variable `turn` to break the tie when both processes want to enter.
[x] Even on a single-core machine, both processes can end up spinning forever.
---
「互斥成立」對：先進去的 $P_i$ 一定是先舉旗、再看到對方的旗子是放下的；之後對方舉旗再檢查，一定看到 $P_i$ 的旗子，只能等。

「兩人都先舉旗就永遠互等」對：兩個旗子都是 true，兩人都卡在 while，違反 progress。

「把兩行對調」錯：兩人可能都先看到對方旗子是 false，再各自舉旗，一起進去，互斥被破壞。

「Peterson 加了 `turn` 打破對稱」對：兩人都想進時，看 `turn` 決定誰讓。

「單核也會永遠空轉」對：單核照樣會在兩人都舉旗之後切換，兩人輪流在 while 裡空轉。

@q sy2-10 | os-sync#2 | 3 | 證明
證明 Peterson 演算法滿足互斥。
---
設 P0、P1 同時在臨界區。兩者都通過了 while，所以 flag[0]=flag[1]=true。P0 通過代表當時 flag[1]=false 或 turn=0；P1 通過代表 flag[0]=false 或 turn=1。因為兩個旗子都是 true，只能是 P0 看到 turn=0、P1 看到 turn=1。turn 只有一個值，最後寫 turn 的那個（例如 P1 寫了 turn=0）會看到 turn 等於對方編號而必須等待，除非對方已經放下旗子。矛盾。

@q sy2-11 | os-sync#2 | 2 | 情境
現代 CPU 可能把「寫 flag」和「讀 flag[j]」的順序對調（為了效能）。照原樣寫的 Peterson 會怎樣？要加什麼？
[x] 可能兩個都進入臨界區；要加特殊指令強制記憶體讀寫照程式順序完成
[ ] 沒影響
[ ] 會變慢但正確
[ ] 編譯器會自動修正
---
實務上直接用硬體原子指令或系統提供的鎖，不用自己寫 Peterson。

@q sy2-12 | os-sync#2 | 2 | 計算
Peterson 中 P0 在 while 迴圈裡等待時，P1 最多可以進入臨界區幾次，P0 就一定能進去？
== 1
---
P1 出來時放下旗子；若 P1 想再進，必須再設 turn=0，這時 P0 的 while 條件 `turn==1` 不成立，P0 就能進。bounded waiting 的上限是 1。

@q sy3-19 | os-sync#3 | 3 | 比較
Compare Peterson's solution with a spinlock built from the atomic `test_and_set` instruction:
`while (test_and_set(&lock)) ;  /* critical section */  lock = false;`
Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[ ] Peterson's solution, as usually presented, works for any number of processes.
[x] On a modern processor that may reorder memory reads and writes, Peterson's solution can fail unless that reordering is prevented.
[ ] The `test_and_set` spinlock shown above guarantees bounded waiting.
[x] The `test_and_set` spinlock shown above provides mutual exclusion for any number of processes.
[ ] Peterson's solution needs a special atomic hardware instruction in order to work.
---
「Peterson 適用任意多個行程」錯：`turn` 只能在兩個人之間讓來讓去，課本的版本只適用兩個行程（推廣要用麵包店演算法）。

「記憶體重排會讓 Peterson 失效」對：它假設「先寫 flag 再讀對方的 flag」照順序發生；硬體把讀提到寫前面，兩人可能都看到對方的 flag 是 false 而一起進去，要用記憶體屏障禁止重排。

「`test_and_set` 鎖保證 bounded waiting」錯：誰搶到全憑運氣，某個行程可能一直搶輸。要加一個 waiting 陣列、離開時依序把鎖交給下一個等待者才行。

「`test_and_set` 鎖對任意多個行程都互斥」對：讀舊值和寫 true 是不可分割的，同一時間只有一個人會讀到 false。

「Peterson 需要特殊硬體指令」錯：它是純軟體解法，只用一般的讀寫；這正是它跟 `test_and_set` 的差別。

@q sy2-14 | os-sync#2 | 1 | 觀念
Which of the following statements about the shared variables in Peterson's solution are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] It uses two shared variables: the array flag[2] and turn.
[x] It uses neither semaphores nor any special hardware instruction.
[x] It needs only ordinary reads and writes of these variables.
[x] flag[i] = true means that process $P_i$ wants to enter its critical section.
[x] When both processes want to enter, turn decides which one goes first.
---
「flag[2] 和 turn」對：只用 turn 就是嚴格輪流，違反 progress。

「不用號誌、不用特殊硬體指令」對、「只要一般讀寫」對：這是它在理論上的意義。

「flag[i] 表示想進」對。

「兩人都想進時 turn 決定誰先」對：turn 表示「讓給誰」。五句都對。

@q sy2-15 | os-sync#2 | 2 | 追蹤
Peterson：P0 和 P1 都已經 `flag=true`，接著 P1 先寫 `turn=0`、P0 再寫 `turn=1`。誰先進臨界區？
== P1
---
最後寫 turn 的是 P0（turn=1），P0 讓步。P1 的條件 `flag[0] && turn==0` 為假，P1 進入。

@q sy2-16 | os-sync#2 | 3 | 陷阱
「Peterson 演算法能推廣到 3 個行程，只要把 `turn` 改成 0、1、2 輪流」對嗎？
[ ] 對
[x] 不對
---
直接把 turn 換成三值會破壞互斥或 progress；多個行程要用 filter lock 或 bakery 演算法（抽號碼牌）這類更複雜的設計。

@q sy3-07 | os-sync#3 | 2 | 計算
`v=5` 時呼叫 `compare_and_swap(&v, 5, 9)`，回傳值和之後的 `v`？
== 5,9
?? 回傳,v
---
回傳原本的值 5；因為等於期望值 5，所以換成 9。（期望值不符時就不換、回傳目前值。）

@q sy3-08 | os-sync#3 | 3 | 情境
A lock-free stack pops by reading top $=x$ and $x$'s next node $y$, then executing CAS(&top, $x$, $y$). Between the read and the CAS, another thread pops $x$, pops $y$ (and frees $y$), and then pushes $x$ back. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[ ] The CAS succeeds only if no other thread has touched the stack since the read.
[ ] After the CAS, top points to $x$ again, so the stack is still consistent and no node has been lost.
[ ] The CAS fails, because the stack changed between the read and the CAS.
[x] Pairing the pointer with a version counter that every update increments makes this CAS fail.
[ ] This problem can only happen on a single-core system.
---
「沒人碰過才會成功」錯、「CAS 會失敗」錯：CAS 只比較「值是不是 $x$」，看不出中間被改過又改回來，所以這裡會成功。這就是 ABA 問題。

「top 又指向 $x$，堆疊一致」錯：CAS 把 top 設成 $y$，而 $y$ 已經不在堆疊裡、還被釋放了，之後再存取就是使用已釋放的記憶體。

「加版本號就會失敗」對：指標雖然一樣，版本號已經變了。

「只會在單核發生」錯：多核上兩個執行緒真的同時跑，更容易發生。

@q sy3-09 | os-sync#3 | 2 | 計算
`fetch_and_add(&x, 3)` 原子地把 x 加 3、回傳**加之前**的值。x 原本 10，三條執行緒各呼叫一次，三個回傳值（由小到大）和最後的 x？
== 10 13 16,19
?? 三個回傳值,最後x
---
不論順序，三個回傳值一定是 10、13、16（各拿到不同的），最後 19。號碼牌（ticket）鎖就是用它發號碼。

@q sy3-10 | os-sync#3 | 3 | 觀念
A ticket lock enters with my = fetch_and_add(&next, 1); while (serving != my) wait; and leaves with serving++. Which of the following statements comparing it with a test_and_set spinlock are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Threads enter in the order in which they asked, so bounded waiting is guaranteed.
[ ] It needs no atomic instruction.
[ ] A waiting thread does not use the CPU.
[x] With a test_and_set spinlock, one thread may keep losing to the others.
[ ] fetch_and_add may give two threads the same number if they call it at the same moment.
---
「照申請順序」對：像抽號碼牌，公平。

「不需要原子指令」錯：fetch_and_add 本身就是原子指令。

「等待時不佔 CPU」錯：還是在迴圈空轉。

「test_and_set 可能一直搶不到」對：誰搶到算誰的。

「同時呼叫可能拿到同號」錯：fetch_and_add 是原子地讀出並加 1，同時呼叫也會一個接一個，號碼一定不同。

@q sy3-11 | os-sync#3 | 2 | 找錯
A thread tries to add 1 to a shared integer $v$ by executing old = v; compare_and_swap(&v, old, old + 1); once, without checking the result. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[ ] If the CAS fails because another thread changed $v$ in between, the CAS waits and then adds 1 anyway.
[x] The fix is to retry in a loop: re-read $v$ and repeat the CAS until it succeeds.
[ ] A single CAS can add 2 to $v$.
[ ] A single CAS can block the thread forever.
[ ] With the retry loop, if two threads each add 1 to $v=5$, the final value may still be 6.
---
「失敗後等一下仍會加 1」錯：CAS 發現 $v$ 不是 old 就什麼都不做、立刻回傳，這次加 1 就不見了。

「用迴圈重試」對：do { old = v; } while (compare_and_swap(&v, old, old + 1) != old);

「會加 2」錯：一次 CAS 只會寫 old + 1。

「會卡住」錯：CAS 不會等待，成功或失敗都立刻回來。

「有重試仍可能是 6」錯：每次成功的 CAS 都是在「讀到的值沒被改過」時才寫入，兩次都會算到，一定是 7。

@q sy3-12 | os-sync#3 | 2 | 比較
多核心上，`test_and_set` 鎖的等待者一直執行 test_and_set（每次都會寫記憶體），和「先讀到 lock==0 才去 test_and_set」比？
[x] 後者好：只讀時各核心用自己的快取，不會一直搶著寫同一個位置、把快取線傳來傳去
[ ] 前者好
[ ] 一樣
[ ] 後者不正確
---
效能差異來自快取一致性的流量。正確性兩者一樣（真正搶鎖時還是用原子指令）。

@q sy3-13 | os-sync#3 | 3 | 陷阱
Which of the following statements about protecting a critical section by disabling interrupts are correct? Select all that apply.
[ ] A user program can protect its critical sections by disabling interrupts.
[x] Disabling interrupts is a privileged operation.
[x] Keeping interrupts disabled for long makes the system unable to respond.
[x] On a multiprocessor, disabling interrupts on one CPU does not stop other CPUs from entering the critical section.

---
「使用者程式可以用」錯：關中斷是特權操作，使用者程式不能執行。

「是特權操作」對：使用者程式不能執行。

「關太久系統無法回應」對：計時器、I/O 中斷都進不來。

「多處理器上擋不住別顆 CPU」對：中斷只是本顆的。

「核心可以用來保護很短的區段」對：單處理器上沒有中斷就不會被換走。

@q sy3-14 | os-sync#3 | 2 | 計算
`lock` 原本 0（沒鎖）。A 呼叫 `test_and_set(&lock)`、接著 B 也呼叫。各得到什麼回傳值？誰拿到鎖？
== 0 1,A
?? A、B 的回傳,誰拿到
---
test_and_set 回傳舊值並設成 1。A 拿到 0（原本沒鎖 → 拿到）；B 拿到 1（已被鎖 → 繼續等）。

@q sy3-15 | os-sync#3 | 2 | 觀念
「原子指令」（atomic）的意思？
[x] 這個讀-改-寫動作整個一起完成，中途不會被其他處理器或中斷插進來
[ ] 很快的指令
[ ] 只能在核心執行的指令
[ ] 不會被快取的指令
---
硬體保證（例如鎖住匯流排或快取線），是所有軟體鎖的基礎。

@q sy3-16 | os-sync#3 | 3 | 情境
On a 64-core machine, every core spins on the same lock with `while (test_and_set(&lock)) ;`. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Each `test_and_set` writes to the lock variable, so even while the lock is held, the spinning cores keep taking the lock's cache block away from each other.
[x] Spinning first with ordinary reads until the lock looks free, and only then calling `test_and_set`, reduces this traffic while the lock is held.
[ ] When the lock is released, the core that has waited the longest is guaranteed to get it next.
[x] In a queue-based lock where each waiter spins on its own variable, releasing the lock notifies only the next waiter.
[x] The spinning can slow down memory accesses made by other cores that never touch the lock.
---
「每次 test_and_set 都是寫，快取區塊被搶來搶去」對：寫入要先拿到獨占權，64 個核心不停地互相作廢對方的副本，匯流排／互連塞滿。

「先用一般讀等到看起來空閒再 test_and_set」對：這叫 test-and-test-and-set。鎖被占用期間大家只讀自己快取裡的副本，不產生流量；只有放鎖那一刻才一擁而上。

「等最久的保證拿到」錯：誰搶到全憑運氣，`test_and_set` 鎖不保證順序，也不保證 bounded waiting。

「排隊式的鎖只通知下一個」對：例如 MCS 鎖，每個等待者在自己的節點上空轉，放鎖只改下一個人的變數，不會一擁而上。

「也會拖慢不碰鎖的核心」對：同步流量占用共享的互連，其他核心的正常記憶體存取也會變慢。

@q sy3-17 | os-sync#3 | 2 | 證明
用 test_and_set 寫鎖，說明它滿足互斥。
---
進入區 `while (test_and_set(&lock));`。test_and_set 原子地回傳舊值並設成 1。第一個在 lock=0 時執行的人拿到 0 而離開迴圈，同時 lock 變 1；在它離開臨界區設 `lock=0` 之前，其他人執行 test_and_set 都拿到 1，只能繼續等。原子性保證不會有兩個人同時拿到 0。

@q sy4-09 | os-sync#4 | 2 | 計算
號誌 S 初值 2（值可以變負，負數代表等待人數）。依序：wait、wait、wait、wait、signal、signal、signal。最後 S 的值和還在等的行程數？
== 1,0
?? S,等待數
---
四次 wait：2→1→0→−1→−2（兩個在等）。三次 signal：−2→−1（叫醒一個）、−1→0（再叫醒一個）、0→1。

@q sy4-10 | os-sync#4 | 2 | 計算
三個行程要依 A → B → C 的順序執行各自的一行。最少要幾個號誌？初值？
== 2,0
?? 號誌數,初值
---
S1、S2 初值 0：A 後 `signal(S1)`；B 前 `wait(S1)`、後 `signal(S2)`；C 前 `wait(S2)`。

@q sy4-11 | os-sync#4 | 3 | 情境
兩條執行緒各自做完第一階段後要「互相等到對方也做完」，才能進第二階段（會合）。用兩個初值 0 的號誌 aDone、bDone，A 該怎麼寫？
[x] `signal(aDone); wait(bDone);`
[ ] `wait(bDone); signal(aDone);`
[ ] `wait(aDone); signal(bDone);`
[ ] `signal(aDone); signal(bDone);`
---
B 寫對稱的 `signal(bDone); wait(aDone);`。如果兩邊都先 wait 再 signal，兩邊都卡住。

@q sy4-12 | os-sync#4 | 2 | 計算
有限緩衝區 5 格，empty 初值 5、full 初值 0。生產者放了 3 次、消費者拿了 1 次（都已完成）。empty 和 full？
== 3,2
?? empty,full
---
放一次：empty−1、full+1；拿一次：full−1、empty+1。$5-3+1=3$、$0+3-1=2$。兩者和永遠是 5（沒有人正在放或拿時）。

@q sy4-13 | os-sync#4 | 3 | 比較
In the first readers–writers solution, readers share a counter `readcount`: the first reader to arrive calls `wait(rw)`, the last reader to leave calls `signal(rw)`, and every update of `readcount` is protected by a separate semaphore `mutex`. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Without `mutex`, concurrent updates of `readcount` could be lost, letting a writer enter while a reader is still reading.
[ ] Every reader calls `wait(rw)` on entry and `signal(rw)` on exit, so readers can read only one at a time, just like writers.
[ ] The semaphore `mutex` is there to give writers priority over readers.
[x] In this solution, writers may starve if readers keep arriving.
[ ] `readcount` needs no protection, because only readers ever change it.
---
「沒有 `mutex` 會遺失更新、寫者跟讀者同時進去」對：兩個讀者都讀到 0、都寫回 1，兩人都以為自己是第一個，都去 `wait(rw)`（第二個會被卡住）；之後第一個離開時減到 0，以為自己是最後一個而 `signal(rw)`，寫者就能進來，可是另一個讀者還在讀。

「每個讀者都 wait、signal `rw`」錯：只有第一個讀者 wait、最後一個 signal，中間的讀者不碰 `rw`，所以很多讀者可以同時讀。

「`mutex` 讓寫者優先」錯：它只保護 `readcount` 這個共享計數器。

「寫者可能飢餓」對：只要一直有讀者進來，`readcount` 就不會回到 0，`rw` 永遠不會被放。

「只有讀者改，所以不用保護」錯：讀者之間也是好幾條執行緒在改同一個變數，一樣有競爭條件。

@q sy4-14 | os-sync#4 | 2 | 情境
哲學家用餐：規定「偶數號先拿左邊、奇數號先拿右邊」。為什麼能避免大家卡住？
[x] 打破了「每個人都拿著一根等下一根」形成的環：相鄰兩人會先搶同一根筷子
[ ] 讓大家輪流吃
[ ] 減少筷子
[ ] 不能避免
---
其他解法：最多讓 4 個人同時拿筷子；或規定兩根都拿得到才拿。

@q sy4-15 | os-sync#4 | 2 | 找錯

[x] 消費者拿著 mutex 卡在 `wait(full)`，生產者進不了 mutex 放東西，雙方永遠卡住
[ ] 正常
[ ] 消費者拿到空資料
[ ] 生產者會覆蓋資料
---
要先 `wait(full)` 再 `wait(mutex)`：先確定有東西才去搶鎖。

@q sy4-16 | os-sync#4 | 3 | 計算
停車場 10 格，用計數號誌 spots（初值 10）控制入場。某時刻 spots 的值是 −3。場內有幾台車、門口幾台在等？

值是負的代表 10 格都滿了，另外 3 台在 wait 裡等。

@q sy4-17 | os-sync#4 | 3 | 陷阱
Which of the following statements about the readers-writers problem are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[ ] In the first readers-writers solution, a writer can never wait forever, because every reader eventually leaves the critical section.
[ ] A writer waits for long only because its priority is too high.
[ ] In the second readers-writers solution, which favors writers, no process can starve.
[x] A fair solution makes readers and writers queue in their order of arrival, for example with one extra semaphore.
[ ] Starvation cannot happen in any solution that uses semaphores.
---
「寫者不會永遠等」錯：第一類是讀者優先。每個讀者都會離開，但新讀者可以在別人還在讀時進來，讀者數可能永遠不回到 0，寫者就一直等。

「寫者優先權太高」錯：剛好相反。

「第二類沒人會餓死」錯：寫者優先時，換讀者可能餓死。

「照到達順序排隊」對：用一個排隊號誌讓大家照順序。

「用號誌就不會餓死」錯：第一、二類都會。

@q sy4-18 | os-sync#4 | 2 | 觀念
用號誌實作互斥時，號誌初值要設多少？用來表示「某事件發生了」時呢？
== 1,0
?? 互斥,事件
---
互斥：1（一次一個）；事件通知：0（還沒發生，等的人要卡住直到 signal）。

@q sy4-19 | os-sync#4 | 3 | 證明
有限緩衝區生產者消費者中，說明 empty＋full 在「沒有人正在放或拿」時永遠等於緩衝區大小 $n$。
---
初始 empty=$n$、full=0。生產者每放一次：`wait(empty)` 讓 empty 減 1，放完 `signal(full)` 讓 full 加 1；消費者對稱。每個完整的放或拿都讓一個減 1、另一個加 1，總和不變，所以永遠是 $n$。正在放或拿的中間時刻，總和會暫時少 1。

@q sy5-06 | os-sync#5 | 3 | 找錯
Mesa 語意的監視器，消費者寫 `if (count == 0) notEmpty.wait();` 然後拿東西。可能出什麼錯？
[x] 被叫醒後到真正重新進入監視器之間，別的消費者可能先把東西拿走；醒來時 count 又是 0，卻直接去拿
[ ] 不會出錯
[ ] 所有消費者都卡住
[ ] 生產者會被卡住
---
Mesa 的 signal 只是「提示」，醒來後要重新檢查條件，所以用 `while`。Hoare 語意立刻把監視器交給被叫醒的人，才能用 `if`。

@q sy5-07 | os-sync#5 | 2 | 比較
條件變數的 `signal()` 在沒有人等的時候會怎樣？號誌的 `signal()` 呢？
[x] 條件變數：什麼都不做（訊號消失）；號誌：值加 1（被記住，之後的 wait 不用等）
[ ] 兩個都被記住
[ ] 兩個都消失
[ ] 條件變數會被記住，號誌消失
---
所以條件變數一定要搭配一個共享狀態（例如 count）來判斷要不要等。

@q sy5-08 | os-sync#5 | 2 | 觀念
A monitor's condition variable supports signal (wake one waiter) and broadcast (wake all waiters). Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Broadcast is useful when one change of state may let several waiters continue.
[x] Broadcast is useful when it is unclear which waiter should be woken.
[ ] Even if every waiter rechecks its condition in a while loop, waking too many waiters can corrupt the shared state.
[ ] Broadcast is never needed.
[ ] If waiters check their condition with if instead of while, broadcast is always safe.
---
「一次改變讓好幾個可以繼續」對：例如記憶體配置器釋放一大塊，好幾個等待不同大小的請求可能都滿足了。

「不確定叫誰」對。

「配合 while 仍會弄壞狀態」錯：被叫醒的會在監視器內再檢查一次，不滿足就繼續等；叫太多只是浪費時間，不會出錯。

「永遠不需要」錯。

「用 if 也安全」錯：被叫醒後條件可能已經不成立了，直接往下做就錯。

@q sy5-09 | os-sync#5 | 2 | 計算
監視器裡有 5 個行程在呼叫監視器的程序：1 個正在執行、3 個在某個條件變數上 wait、1 個剛到在入口排隊。此刻「在監視器裡面執行」的有幾個？
== 1
---
監視器保證一次只有一個在執行；在條件上 wait 的已經放開監視器，不算在執行。

@q sy5-10 | os-sync#5 | 2 | 觀念
行程在監視器內呼叫 `x.wait()` 時，監視器的鎖？
[x] 自動放開，讓別人能進來改變條件；被叫醒時要重新拿到鎖才繼續
[ ] 一直拿著
[ ] 永久釋放
[ ] 交給下一個等 x 的人
---
如果拿著鎖睡覺，能讓條件成立的人永遠進不來。pthread 的 `pthread_cond_wait(&c, &m)` 也是同時放開 mutex 並睡覺。

@q sy5-11 | os-sync#5 | 3 | 情境
A monitor limits a resource to at most 3 users: enter() waits on condition $c$ while inUse == 3, and leave() does inUse-- and then $c$.signal(). The monitor uses signal-and-continue (Mesa) semantics. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] enter() must use while (inUse == 3) c.wait(); because by the time a woken thread runs, another thread may already have taken the free place.
[x] The while loop also protects against wakeups that happen without any signal.
[x] Using if instead of while can let a fourth user in.
[x] Each c.wait() releases the monitor while the thread sleeps.
[x] Under Hoare semantics, where the signaled thread runs immediately, if would be enough, assuming no spurious wakeups.
---
「醒來時名額可能已被搶走」對：Mesa 語意下被叫醒的只是回到可執行，不是立刻執行。

「也處理假性喚醒」對：某些系統允許無緣無故醒來。

「用 if 可能放進第四個人」對：醒來後不再檢查，名額已被搶走也照樣進去。

「wait 時放掉監視器」對：wait 會睡覺，不是空轉；放掉監視器別人才能進來呼叫 leave()。

「Hoare 語意下 if 就夠」對：被叫醒的立刻接手，狀態還沒被別人改。五句都對。

@q sy5-12 | os-sync#5 | 2 | 比較
Compare monitors with semaphores for writing synchronization code, such as the bounded-buffer problem. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] In a monitor, mutual exclusion among its procedures is enforced automatically, so the programmer cannot forget to release the lock.
[x] A monitor can be implemented with semaphores, and a semaphore can be implemented with a monitor, so the two are equally expressive.
[x] A process that waits on a condition variable releases the monitor, so other processes can enter while it sleeps.
[ ] In the semaphore solution of the bounded-buffer problem, a producer may swap the order of `wait(empty)` and `wait(mutex)` without any effect on correctness.
[x] With semaphores, if one code path forgets to call `signal(mutex)`, other processes may be blocked forever.
---
「監視器自動互斥、不會忘記放鎖」對：進出由語言／編譯器處理。

「可以互相實作、表達能力相同」對：差別在好不好寫對，不在能力。

「wait 會放掉監視器」對：別人才能進來改變條件、叫醒它；不然一定卡死。

「生產者把 `wait(empty)` 和 `wait(mutex)` 對調沒差」錯：先拿到 mutex 才發現緩衝區滿了而睡著，消費者拿不到 mutex 無法取走東西，兩邊永遠互等，死結。

「忘記 `signal(mutex)` 會讓別人永遠卡住」對：號誌靠程式設計師自己在每條路徑上配對 wait／signal，這正是監視器想避免的錯誤。

@q sy5-13 | os-sync#5 | 3 | 陷阱
Hoare 語意中，P 在監視器裡呼叫 `x.signal()` 叫醒 Q。接下來誰在監視器裡執行？
[x] Q 立刻執行；P 暫停，等 Q 離開或 wait 之後才繼續
[ ] P 繼續執行，Q 等 P 離開
[ ] 兩個同時執行
[ ] 都不執行
---
Mesa（signal-and-continue）是 P 繼續、Q 之後再搶；大多數實際系統用 Mesa，因為實作簡單、少一次 context switch。

@q sy5-14 | os-sync#5 | 2 | 證明
說明為什麼條件變數的 `wait` 必須「放開鎖」和「開始睡覺」一起原子地完成。
---
如果先放鎖、再去睡：中間這一小段，別人可能進來把條件改好並 signal，但這時還沒有人在等（signal 消失），之後才睡下去就永遠沒人叫醒（lost wakeup）。兩步原子完成才能保證「我睡下去之後的 signal 一定叫得到我」。

@q sy5-15 | os-sync#5 | 1 | 觀念
Java 的 `synchronized` 方法加上 `wait()`／`notify()`，最接近哪個概念？
[x] 監視器（物件的鎖＋一個條件變數）
[ ] 號誌
[ ] Peterson
[ ] spinlock
---
`notify` 是 Mesa 語意，所以 Java 也要用 `while` 包 `wait`。

@q sy6-06 | os-sync#6 | 2 | 情境
Two people meet in a corridor; both politely step to the same side, then both step to the other side, and so on, never passing. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] This is a livelock: both keep changing state but make no progress.
[ ] This is a deadlock: both stand still waiting for each other.
[ ] Having both people always retry immediately, with no delay, usually breaks the pattern.
[ ] This is a race condition on a shared variable.
[x] Unlike deadlocked processes, livelocked processes keep using the CPU.
---
「活鎖」對：一直在動，沒有進展。

「死結」錯：死結的人是靜止不動的。

「立刻重試就能打破」錯：兩人一直同步動作，正是卡住的原因。要加隨機延遲，讓兩人同時動的機率變小，乙太網路碰撞後就是這樣做。

「共享變數的競爭條件」錯。

「活鎖會一直吃 CPU」對：它們一直在執行，只是白做工。

@q sy6-07 | os-sync#6 | 3 | 計算
context switch 要 10 µs。臨界區平均只拿 1 µs 就放開。等鎖時用 spinlock 和「睡覺等叫醒」，哪個划算？（多核心）
[x] spinlock：平均只要空轉約 1 µs；睡覺要切換出去再切回來約 20 µs
[ ] 睡覺
[ ] 一樣
[ ] 都不行
---
等待時間比兩次 context switch 短就適合 spin。單核心上 spin 沒意義：拿鎖的人在你轉的時候根本沒在跑。

@q sy6-08 | os-sync#6 | 3 | 陷阱
單核心電腦上用 spinlock 等一把被別的執行緒拿著的鎖，會怎樣？
[x] 空轉直到時間片用完；拿鎖的執行緒在你轉的時候不會前進，純浪費
[ ] 很快就拿到
[ ] 跟多核心一樣好
[ ] 會死結
---
所以單核心上的鎖等待應該讓出 CPU。核心裡用 spinlock 時會先關搶先，避免這種情況。

@q sy6-09 | os-sync#6 | 2 | 比較
Which of the following statements comparing deadlock and starvation are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[ ] In a deadlock, the processes keep running but never make any progress.
[ ] In starvation, no process in the system makes any progress.
[ ] Starvation always implies deadlock.
[x] Aging can prevent starvation in priority scheduling.
[ ] A deadlock can consist of a single process waiting for nothing.
---
「死結時行程一直在跑」錯：那是活鎖。死結是一組行程各自等組內另一個行程，全部停住不動。

「飢餓時整個系統都沒進展」錯：飢餓時系統有進展，只是某個人一直輪不到。

「飢餓一定伴隨死結」錯：別人都在前進。

「aging 防飢餓」對：逐漸提高久等行程的優先權。飢餓靠公平性解，死結要打破互相等待。

「一個行程什麼都不等也算死結」錯：死結需要在等待某個不會發生的事件。

@q sy6-10 | os-sync#6 | 3 | 情境
優先權繼承：低優先權 L 拿著鎖，高優先權 H 來等這把鎖。L 的優先權暫時變成多少？什麼時候恢復？
[x] 升到 H 的優先權；放開這把鎖時恢復原本的
[ ] 升到最高；永遠不恢復
[ ] 不變
[ ] 降到最低；H 拿到鎖時恢復
---
這樣中優先權的工作搶不走 L，L 趕快做完放鎖，H 等待的時間就有上限。

@q sy6-11 | os-sync#6 | 2 | 計算
優先權天花板協定：一把鎖可能被優先權 2、5、7（數字大＝優先）的工作使用。任何工作拿到這把鎖時，優先權立刻升到多少？
== 7
---
天花板＝所有可能使用者中最高的優先權。比繼承更早升級，可以順便避免某些死結。

@q sy6-12 | os-sync#6 | 2 | 找錯
學生說：「程式沒有死結，所以每個執行緒最後一定會做完。」反例？
[x] 讀者優先的讀者寫者：讀者源源不絕時，寫者一直等（飢餓），但系統沒有死結
[ ] 哲學家用餐
[ ] 只有一條執行緒的程式
[ ] 正確的 Peterson
---
沒有死結只保證「整體有進展」，不保證「每個人都有進展」。

@q sy6-13 | os-sync#6 | 3 | 情境
Thread $T_1$ acquires lock `m1` and then lock `m2`, while thread $T_2$ acquires `m2` and then `m1`. A programmer changes both threads so that if the second lock is not available, the thread releases the first lock and immediately tries again from the beginning. In a test run on a machine with 2 cores, both threads start at time 0, and each attempt takes about 1 microsecond. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The original code can deadlock.
[ ] After the change, at least one thread is guaranteed to enter its critical section within a bounded time.
[ ] After the change, the threads can still deadlock.
[x] Making both threads acquire `m1` before `m2` removes the possibility of deadlock.
[ ] The situation created by the change is harmless, because neither thread is blocked and the CPU is always busy.
---
「原本會死結」對：$T_1$ 拿 `m1`、$T_2$ 拿 `m2`，各等對方，形成循環等待。

「改了之後保證有人很快進去」錯：兩邊動作剛好同步時，每次都在同一刻發現拿不到、放掉、重來，可能一直拿一把、放一把，誰都進不去。這是活鎖。

「改了還會死結」錯：拿不到就放，不再「占有並等待」，死結的必要條件被打破了。

「固定先 `m1` 後 `m2`」對：所有人照同一個順序拿鎖，就不可能形成循環等待，這是最根本的解法。

「活鎖無害」錯：CPU 一直在忙，卻沒有任何進展，跟卡住一樣糟。要加隨機等待，或改成固定順序。

@q sy6-14 | os-sync#6 | 2 | 觀念
下列哪一種情況屬於「飢餓」？
[x] 優先權排程中，低優先權的行程因為高優先權的一直進來而永遠排不到
[ ] 兩個行程各拿一把鎖互等
[ ] 兩個行程同時改共享變數
[ ] 行程在等使用者輸入
---
第二個是死結；第三個是競爭條件；第四個是正常的 waiting。

@q sy6-15 | os-sync#6 | 3 | 比較
mutex（會睡覺的鎖）和 spinlock 在**中斷處理程式**裡，能用哪一種？
[x] 只能用 spinlock：中斷處理程式不能睡覺（它不是一個可以被排程的行程）
[ ] 只能用 mutex
[ ] 兩個都可以
[ ] 兩個都不行
---
所以核心裡跟中斷處理程式共享的資料，會用 spinlock＋關中斷保護。

@q sy6-16 | os-sync#6 | 2 | 計算
一把鎖的平均持有時間 50 µs，context switch 10 µs。多核心上該 spin 還是睡覺？
== 睡覺
---
空轉約 50 µs 比兩次切換約 20 µs 浪費，應該讓出 CPU。實務上常用混合：先 spin 一小段，拿不到再睡。

@q sy6-17 | os-sync#6 | 3 | 證明
說明為什麼「單核心上，只要拿鎖時關掉搶先（preemption），spinlock 就不會空轉」。
---
單核心上 spin 的人在等另一個執行者放鎖，那個執行者只有在 CPU 上才能前進。拿鎖的人關掉搶先後，在放鎖之前不會被換下，所以其他人根本不會在它持鎖期間跑到 spin 的地方；一旦它放鎖、打開搶先，別人才有機會執行，這時鎖已經是空的。

@q dl1-07 | os-deadlock#1 | 2 | 情境
A bank's database runs thousands of transactions per second, and each transaction locks the rows it updates until it commits. One afternoon, the dashboard shows two transactions frozen for 30 seconds: T1 has locked row 5 and is waiting for row 9, while T2 has locked row 9 and is waiting for row 5; neither can commit, and no other transaction holds either row. The developers discuss several remedies: making every transaction lock the rows it needs in increasing order of row number, letting the database look for such cycles of waiting transactions every 5 seconds and abort one of them, or giving T1 a higher priority than T2. They also ask what happens to the work of an aborted transaction, which may have run for several seconds and updated many other rows. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Requiring every transaction to lock rows in increasing order of row number prevents this situation.
[x] That rule breaks the circular-wait condition.
[x] Databases often instead detect such cycles and abort one of the transactions.
[x] The aborted transaction can be restarted later.
[ ] Giving T1 a higher priority prevents all such deadlocks.
---
「照列號由小到大鎖」時 T2 也要先鎖第 5 列，不可能互等，打破「循環等待」。資料庫常改用「偵測後中止」其中一個交易，被中止的交易之後「可以重跑」。四句都對。優先權不會讓已經互相持有的鎖解開。

@q dl1-08 | os-deadlock#1 | 3 | 觀念
An office has 1 printer shared by 40 employees. Instead of letting programs talk to the printer directly, the operating system uses spooling: each program writes its whole output to a queue of files on disk, and a single printer daemon, the only process that ever holds the printer, prints the jobs one after another. The disk queue can hold about 2 GB of jobs. A student analyzes which of the four deadlock conditions this design breaks, and whether the same idea works for other resources, such as a lock that protects a shared data structure. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] It breaks mutual exclusion for the printer: programs share a disk queue instead of holding the printer.
[x] A lock cannot be handled this way, because it must be mutually exclusive by nature.
[ ] It breaks the hold-and-wait condition.
[ ] It breaks the circular-wait condition.
[ ] It removes every possibility of waiting, even when the disk queue is full.
---
程式不再直接佔用印表機，而是共用一個可以同時寫入的佇列，打破的是「互斥」；鎖本來就要互斥，「不能這樣處理」。持有並等待、循環等待都不是它處理的對象。磁碟佇列滿了，行程又會變成在等磁碟空間。

@q dl1-09 | os-deadlock#1 | 2 | 陷阱
A resource manager allows 8 processes to share 3 tape drives, 2 plotters, and 1 scanner. To prevent deadlock, it enforces the following rule: a process that holds some resources and needs more must first release everything it holds, and then request all the resources it needs together in a single request, which is granted only when all of them are free at once. A video-editing job, for example, holds a tape drive for 20 minutes and then needs the scanner as well, so it must release the tape drive and ask for both again. The administrators want to know which deadlock condition the rule breaks, whether deadlock is then impossible, and what the rule costs in utilization and fairness, since some jobs need all 3 tape drives at once and others need only the scanner. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The rule breaks the hold-and-wait condition.
[x] Resource utilization becomes low.
[x] A process that needs many popular resources may starve.
[x] Deadlock can no longer occur under this rule.
[ ] The rule breaks the mutual-exclusion condition.
---
申請新資源時手上什麼都沒有，就不會「持有並等待」，所以「死結不再發生」。代價是資源常常閒置、「使用率低」，而且要一次湊齊很多熱門資源的行程「可能飢餓」。四句都對。資源仍然一次只給一個行程用，互斥沒變。

@q dl1-10 | os-deadlock#1 | 1 | 計算
要形成循環等待，最少要幾個行程？
== 2
---
P1 等 P2 拿著的、P2 等 P1 拿著的。（單一行程對同一把不可重入的鎖鎖兩次也會卡住，但那不是行程之間的循環等待。）

@q dl1-11 | os-deadlock#1 | 2 | 情境
Two students each borrow one volume of a two-volume reference book from the library: Ann has volume 1 and needs volume 2 to finish her report, while Bo has volume 2 and needs volume 1. The library has only one copy of each volume, a volume can be held by only one reader at a time, the library never takes a book back from a reader before the reader returns it, and neither student will return a volume first. A student of operating systems models the situation as a deadlock between 2 processes and 2 resources. Which of the four deadlock conditions hold? Select all that apply.
[x] Mutual exclusion
[x] Hold and wait
[x] No preemption
[x] Circular wait
[ ] None of the four conditions
---
一本書一次只能一人借（互斥）、拿著一冊等另一冊（持有並等待）、不能強迫對方還（不可搶占）、互相等對方（循環等待），四個都成立，所以是死結。

@q dl1-12 | os-deadlock#1 | 2 | 找錯
A student says: "To prevent deadlock, at least two of the four conditions must be broken," and proposes a design for a system with 12 kinds of resources that both orders all locks and makes every process request its resources at once. The four conditions are mutual exclusion, hold and wait, no preemption, and circular wait, and a deadlock can occur only if all four hold at the same time. A reviewer points out that each extra rule costs performance, for example about 15% lower resource utilization and 8% lower throughput for the all-at-once rule in their tests, and asks the student to reconsider the claim. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] All four conditions are necessary for deadlock.
[x] Breaking any one of them prevents deadlock.
[x] So breaking two of them is never required.
[x] Systems usually break the cheapest one, often circular wait by ordering resources.
[x] Mutual exclusion is often the hardest to break, since many resources cannot be shared.
---
四個都是「必要條件」，少一個死結就不可能發生，所以「打破一個就夠」，學生說要兩個是錯的。實務上挑「最便宜的」打破，通常是給資源編號照順序申請；很多資源本質上不能共用，「互斥最難打破」。五句都對。

@q dl1-13 | os-deadlock#1 | 3 | 比較
The no-preemption condition can be broken by forcibly taking a resource back from the process that holds it and giving it back later. An operating-systems class considers 5 resources: the CPU, which the scheduler can take away at a timer interrupt every 10 ms; pages of main memory, which can be written to disk and brought back; a printer halfway through a 20-page document; a file that a process has half written; and a mutex that protects a data structure in the middle of an update. For which of the following resources is this suitable? Select all that apply.
[x] The CPU
[x] Main memory, whose pages can be swapped out
[ ] A printer in the middle of printing a document
[ ] A file that is half written
[ ] A mutex protecting a half-updated data structure
---
可以把狀態存起來、之後還原的資源才適合：CPU 本來就靠 context switch 搶占；記憶體可以 swap 出去。印表機印到一半、寫到一半的檔案、正在修改中的資料結構，搶走就壞了。

@q dl1-14 | os-deadlock#1 | 2 | 情境
A mountain road has a tunnel only 1 car wide and 300 m long, carrying about 200 cars per hour, with no room to pass inside. One morning, cars enter from both ends at the same time, and the two lines of cars get stuck facing each other in the middle; each line holds the part of the tunnel it occupies and waits for the part held by the other. The police resolve the situation by making the line from the north side back out entirely, so that the south line can pass, and the northern drivers then have to drive the whole tunnel again. A student models the tunnel as a sequence of road segments, each held by one car at a time, and asks which deadlock condition the police action breaks, what it costs, and how the situation could be prevented in the future. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Backing out breaks the no-preemption condition by forcing one side to give up road it holds.
[x] The side that backs out loses the progress it made.
[x] Letting cars enter from only one end at a time would prevent the situation.
[ ] Backing out breaks the mutual-exclusion condition.
[ ] Backing out breaks the hold-and-wait condition.
---
倒車就是把已經佔到的路面「搶回來」，打破「不可搶占」，代價是「進度白費」。一次只放一個方向進來，就不會有面對面互等，能事先避免。路面一次還是只能一台車用，互斥沒變；車子仍然是佔著路等前方，持有並等待也沒變。

@q dl1-15 | os-deadlock#1 | 3 | 陷阱
A thread calls `lock(m)` on an ordinary, non-recursive mutex, and inside the critical section it calls a helper function that, in turn, does `lock(m)` again on the same mutex:
```
void update() { lock(m); helper(); unlock(m); }
void helper() { lock(m); /* ... */ unlock(m); }
```
In a test with 4 threads, the program hangs as soon as any thread calls `update()`. A student wants to explain the hang and to know how libraries support code that needs to lock a mutex it already holds, and whether the system could simply detect the situation. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The thread blocks itself forever, since only it could release `m`.
[x] A recursive mutex lets its owning thread lock it again.
[ ] The second `lock(m)` succeeds, because the thread already owns `m`.
[ ] The system detects the self-wait and lets the thread through.
[ ] With every kind of ordinary mutex, the second call simply returns an error.
---
第二次 lock 要等 m 被放開，但放開的就是自己，「永遠卡住」；這不是典型的多行程死結，但一樣沒有進展。「可重入鎖」記住擁有者和次數，同一條執行緒可以重複上鎖。一般的鎖不記得擁有者，不會讓它通過，系統也不會自動放行；只有特別設定成檢查錯誤的鎖才會回傳錯誤，預設的鎖就是卡住。

@q dl1-16 | os-deadlock#1 | 1 | 觀念
An operating system may take resources away from processes to recover from deadlock or to share them fairly, but only if the preempted process can later continue as if nothing had happened. The system manages a 4-core CPU, 16 GB of main memory divided into 4 KiB pages, a printer, a mutex lock that protects a shared table, and a tape drive. A student must decide which of these resources can be preempted safely at any moment, without the process noticing or producing wrong output. Which of the following resources can be preempted without causing errors? Select all that apply.
[x] The CPU
[x] Pages of main memory, which can be swapped out and brought back
[ ] A printer in the middle of printing
[ ] A mutex lock
[ ] A tape that is half written
---
CPU 被搶走時暫存器存在 PCB，之後完全還原；記憶體頁可以換出再換回，這兩個可以。印到一半的印表機、正在保護資料結構的鎖、寫到一半的磁帶，搶走就出錯。

@q dl1-17 | os-deadlock#1 | 3 | 證明
說明為什麼「循環等待」是死結的必要條件。
---
死結時一群行程都在等，每個等的資源被這群裡的另一個行程拿著（等的是群外的行程的話，那個行程能繼續跑完放資源，就不算死結）。從任一個行程出發沿「等誰」走，每一步都還在這群有限的行程裡，一定會回到走過的某個行程，形成環。

@q dl1-18 | os-deadlock#1 | 2 | 情境
In a Java banking program, method `f` transfers money from account `x` to account `y`, and method `g` transfers money from `y` to `x`; each method locks both accounts before changing them:
```
void f() { synchronized (x) { synchronized (y) { /* move money */ } } }
void g() { synchronized (y) { synchronized (x) { /* move money */ } } }
```
Two threads call `f` and `g` at the same time, about 1000 times per second each, and the two accounts are shared by both threads. In testing, the program usually works, but every few hours both threads freeze and must be killed. A developer wonders whether the Java compiler or the JVM should have caught the problem, how often the freeze can happen, and how the code should be changed so that both methods stay correct and the freeze can never occur. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[x] The threads may get stuck, each holding one lock and waiting for the other.
[ ] The threads always get stuck whenever `f` and `g` run at the same time.
[ ] The Java compiler rejects code that takes locks in different orders.
[ ] Making `g` acquire `y` and then `x` removes the problem.
[ ] The JVM detects the cycle at run time and automatically releases both locks so that both threads continue.
---
一條拿到 `x`、另一條拿到 `y`，各自等對方手上的那把，循環等待成立，「可能卡住」；但要剛好交錯才會發生，不是每次。`g` 現在就是先 `y` 後 `x`，正是問題所在，要改成和 `f` 一樣先 `x` 後 `y`。編譯器不檢查上鎖順序，JVM 也不會自動解開，程式只會停住。

@q dl1-19 | os-deadlock#1 | 2 | 計算
To break circular wait, resources are numbered R1 = 1, R2 = 5, R3 = 8, and must be requested in increasing order. A process currently holds R2. Which of the following statements are correct?
[x] It may request R3.
[x] To obtain R1, it must first release R2.
[x] A process that holds R3 may not request R2.
[x] Every wait then points toward a larger number, so no cycle can form.
[ ] It may request R1 directly, because R1 has the smallest number.
---
只准由小到大申請：手上最大是 5，「可以要 R3」；要 R1（編號 1）必須「先放掉 R2」，不能直接要；拿著 R3（8）的行程「不能再要 R2」（5）。等待的方向永遠是「往大的編號」，不可能繞回來。

@q dl1-20 | os-deadlock#1 | 2 | 比較
Which of the following statements about the difference between a deadlock and a process simply waiting for slow I/O are correct?
[x] A process waiting for I/O eventually gets what it waits for.
[x] In a deadlock, each process waits for another waiting process.
[x] The key test is whether the thing being waited for can make progress on its own.
[ ] A process blocked on slow I/O counts as deadlocked once it waits longer than a fixed timeout.
[ ] Deadlocks can involve only I/O devices, not locks or memory.
---
「等 I/O 早晚會等到」；死結中「被等的對象自己也在等」，永遠等不到，所以關鍵是「被等的東西能不能自己往前走」。等得久不等於死結，判斷看的是有沒有循環等待，不是時間。死結也會發生在鎖、記憶體等任何資源上。

@q dl2-07 | os-deadlock#2 | 2 | 計算
Each resource has a single instance. The resource-allocation graph has edges P1→R1, R1→P2, P2→R2, R2→P3, P3→R1. Which of the following statements are correct?
[x] P2 and P3 are deadlocked.
[x] P1 also waits forever for R1, but it is not on the cycle.
[ ] There is no deadlock, because P1 lies on no cycle.
[ ] P1, P2 and P3 all lie on the cycle.
[ ] Terminating P1 ends the deadlock.
---
環是 P2→R2→P3→R1→P2：P2 等 P3 的 R2，P3 等 P2 的 R1，單一實例時有環就是死結，「P2、P3 死結」。P1 等的 R1 永遠不會被放，所以「P1 也永遠在等」，但它不在環上。終止不在環上的 P1 什麼都沒釋放，環還在。

@q dl2-08 | os-deadlock#2 | 3 | 追蹤
R1 有 2 個實例、R2 有 1 個。P1 拿一個 R1、要 R2；P2 拿 R2、要 R1；P3 拿一個 R1、沒有要別的。圖中有環 P1→R2→P2→R1→P1。有沒有死結？
[x] 沒有：P3 做完放出 R1，P2 拿到後做完放出 R2，P1 也能完成
[ ] 有，因為有環
[ ] 有，P3 也會卡住
[ ] 無法判斷
---
多實例時有環只是**可能**死結，要像這樣模擬「誰能做完、放出資源」才知道。

@q dl2-09 | os-deadlock#2 | 2 | 計算
資源配置圖中，P1 等 P2 拿著的資源、P2 等 P3 拿著的、P3 等 P1 拿著的，P4 等 P2 拿著的。轉成「等待圖」（只留行程節點）後有幾條邊？
== 4
---
每個「P 等 Q 拿著的資源」變成一條 P→Q：P1→P2、P2→P3、P3→P1、P4→P2。單一實例時，等待圖有環 ⇔ 死結。

@q dl2-10 | os-deadlock#2 | 3 | 觀念
In deadlock avoidance with claim edges (dashed edges meaning a process may request a resource in the future), a process actually makes the request. Which of the following statements are correct?
[x] The system pretends to turn the claim edge into an assignment edge and checks for a cycle.
[x] If a cycle would appear, the request waits even if the resource is free right now.
[x] This method works only when every resource type has one instance.
[x] Checking for a cycle takes about $O(n^2)$ time for $n$ processes.
[ ] A request for a resource that is currently free is always granted at once, since nobody holds it.
---
「假設給了，看會不會出現環」；會就先不給，「即使資源現在是空的」，所以「空的就給」錯。環代表死結只在單一實例時成立，「只適用每種資源一個實例」，多實例要用 Banker。圖上找環約要 $n^2$ 的運算量。

@q dl2-11 | os-deadlock#2 | 2 | 情境
Linux、Windows 對一般應用程式的死結大多「不管」（鴕鳥演算法）。合理的理由？
[x] 死結很少發生，而預防、避免、偵測都有固定的效能代價或限制；發生時讓使用者重開程式就好
[ ] 它們不可能死結
[ ] 它們每次申請資源都做安全檢查
[ ] 硬體會處理
---
核心內部自己還是會小心排序拿鎖；資料庫則一定會偵測並中止交易。

@q dl3-21 | os-deadlock#3 | 2 | 比較
Which of the following statements comparing deadlock avoidance (the banker's algorithm) with deadlock detection are correct?
?? 複選
[x] Avoidance needs each process's maximum demand in advance, while detection does not.
[ ] Detection needs each process's maximum demand in advance, while avoidance does not.
[ ] Avoidance requires no computation when a request arrives.
[ ] Detection keeps deadlock from ever forming.
[ ] Detection, like avoidance, must run a check every time any process requests a resource.
---
避免要試算未來最壞情況，所以「要事先知道最大需求」，每次申請都要算；偵測只看目前的配置和請求，不需要最大需求。偵測接受死結發生、事後處理，通常定期或在某些條件下才跑，不必每次申請都檢查。

@q dl2-13 | os-deadlock#2 | 2 | 找錯
A student says: "If the resource-allocation graph has a cycle, there is definitely a deadlock." Which of the following statements are correct?
[x] The claim can fail when a resource on the cycle has several instances.
[x] With single-instance resources, a cycle does imply a deadlock.
[ ] With single-instance resources, a cycle can disappear when a process outside it finishes.
[ ] The claim fails only for cycles of length two.
[ ] The claim holds whenever the cycle contains at least three processes.
---
多實例時，環外的行程放出一份資源就能讓環解開，所以「多實例時可能不成立」；每種資源只有一個實例時，有環 ⇔ 死結。單一實例時環上的資源都在環上的行程手裡，環外的人放不出來。成不成立看的是實例數，不是環的長度。

@q dl2-14 | os-deadlock#2 | 3 | 情境
A transfer must lock two accounts at once, from account X to account Y. Two transfers X→Y and Y→X running at the same time may get stuck. Which of the following statements are correct?
[x] Locking the two accounts in increasing order of account number, whatever the direction of the transfer, prevents this.
[x] Always locking the source account first can make the two opposite transfers form a cycle.
[x] One global lock for all transfers also prevents it, at the cost of concurrency.
[ ] Always locking the destination account first prevents this.
[ ] Always locking the source account first prevents this.
---
「依帳戶編號由小到大上鎖」，兩筆轉帳都先鎖同一個帳戶，就不可能互等。「先鎖轉出帳戶」時 X→Y 先鎖 X、Y→X 先鎖 Y，正好成環；先鎖轉入帳戶也一樣。「一把全域鎖」只有一個資源，不會成環，但所有轉帳只能排隊。

@q dl2-15 | os-deadlock#2 | 1 | 觀念
資源配置圖中，從資源指向行程的邊（R→P）代表？
[x] R 的一個實例已經分配給 P
[ ] P 在申請 R
[ ] P 將來可能申請 R
[ ] P 釋放了 R
---
P→R 是申請邊（在等）；R→P 是指派邊（拿著）。

@q dl2-16 | os-deadlock#2 | 3 | 計算
R1 and R2 have one instance each, and R3 has two. P1 holds R1 and requests R3; P2 holds one R3 and requests R2; P3 holds R2 and requests R1; P4 holds one R3. Which of the following statements are correct?
[x] There is no deadlock.
[x] The graph contains the cycle P1→R3→P2→R2→P3→R1→P1.
[x] P4 waits for nothing, so it can finish and release its R3.
[x] After P4 finishes, P1, P3 and P2 can complete in that order.
[x] The cycle is broken by an instance of R3 held outside the cycle.
---
環確實存在，但 R3 有兩個實例；「P4 不等任何東西」，做完放出一個 R3；P1 拿到完成，放 R1、R3；P3 拿 R1 完成，放 R2；P2 拿 R2 完成，「P1、P3、P2 依序完成」，所以「沒有死結」，解開它的正是「環外那個 R3」。五句都對。

@q dl2-17 | os-deadlock#2 | 2 | 比較
死結的四種處理方式，哪一種**允許**系統走到「無法保證不會死結」的狀態？
[x] 偵測與復原（以及忽略）
[ ] 預防
[ ] 避免
[ ] 四種都不允許
---
預防從結構上讓死結不可能；避免只讓系統留在安全狀態；偵測則讓它發生再處理。

@q dl2-18 | os-deadlock#2 | 3 | 證明
證明：每種資源只有一個實例時，資源配置圖有環 ⇒ 死結。
---
環上每個行程 P 都在等一個資源 R（P→R），R 只有一個實例且已經分給環上的下一個行程 Q（R→Q），Q 又在等下一個。每個行程等的資源都被環上另一個也在等的行程拿著，沒有實例可以從環外來，所以環上的行程永遠等不到，即死結。

@q dl3-10 | os-deadlock#3 | 3 | 計算
Banker：5 個行程、3 種資源。Allocation：P0 (1,0,2)、P1 (2,1,0)、P2 (0,1,1)、P3 (1,0,0)、P4 (0,0,1)；Max：P0 (3,2,3)、P1 (3,2,2)、P2 (1,2,2)、P3 (2,1,3)、P4 (1,1,1)；Available (1,1,1)。依序掃描（每輪從 P0 看到 P4）找到的安全序列？
== P2 P4 P1 P3 P0
== P2,P4,P1,P3,P0
---
Need：P0 (2,2,1)、P1 (1,1,2)、P2 (1,1,1)、P3 (1,1,3)、P4 (1,1,0)。第 1 輪：P2 可（Work→(1,2,2)）、P4 可（→(1,2,3)）；第 2 輪：P1 可（→(3,3,3)）、P3 可（→(4,3,3)）；第 3 輪：P0。程式驗算。

@q dl3-11 | os-deadlock#3 | 3 | 計算
接上題，系統每種資源的總數是多少？這個狀態一共有幾種安全序列？
== 5 3 5,16
?? 總數,安全序列數
---
總數＝Allocation 加總＋Available＝(4,2,4)+(1,1,1)。程式窮舉 120 種順序，16 種安全。安全序列通常不只一種，題目要的是「找到一種」。

@q dl3-12 | os-deadlock#3 | 3 | 計算
Banker's algorithm with Available $=(1,1,1)$. Allocation: P0 (1,0,2), P1 (2,1,0), P2 (0,1,1), P3 (1,0,0), P4 (0,0,1); Max: P0 (3,2,3), P1 (3,2,2), P2 (1,2,2), P3 (2,1,3), P4 (1,1,1). P0 requests $(1,1,1)$. Which of the following statements are correct?
[x] The request satisfies Request ≤ Need and Request ≤ Available.
[x] After pretending to grant it, Available is $(0,0,0)$ and no process can finish, so the state is unsafe.
[x] The banker's algorithm does not grant the request.
[ ] The request is granted, because $(1,1,1)\le$ Available.
[ ] The request is rejected as an error, because together with P0's Allocation it exceeds P0's declared Max.
---
P0 的 Need＝Max−Allocation＝$(2,2,1)$，$(1,1,1)$ 不超過它也不超過 Available，「兩個前提成立」，Allocation 加上請求是 $(2,1,3)\le$ Max，也沒有超過。假設給了 Available 變 $(0,0,0)$，每個行程都還需要至少一個資源，沒有人能完成，「不安全」（程式確認），所以「不給」。「資源夠」只是第一關。

@q dl3-13 | os-deadlock#3 | 2 | 計算
接上題，改成 P4 申請 (1,0,0)。給不給？
== 給
---
(1,0,0) ≤ Need (1,1,0) 且 ≤ Available。假設給了：Available (0,1,1)，P4 的 Need 變 (0,1,0) 可以先完成，放出 (1,0,1) 後 Available (1,1,2)，接著 P1、P2、P3、P0 都能完成。程式驗算安全。

@q dl3-14 | os-deadlock#3 | 2 | 計算
只有一種資源。Available 2；P0 拿 3、最多要 9；P1 拿 2、最多要 4；P2 拿 2、最多要 7。安全嗎？
== 不安全
---
Need：6、2、5。P1 可以完成，Available 變 4；P0 要 6、P2 要 5，都不夠。如果 Available 是 3，P1 完成後變 5，P2 就可以，接著 P0，就安全了。

@q dl3-15 | os-deadlock#3 | 3 | 計算
同一種資源，三個行程最多各需要 3、4、5 個。系統至少要有幾個，才保證無論怎麼申請都不會死結？
== 10
---
最壞情況每人都拿到「最大需求減 1」還在等：$2+3+4=9$；再多 1 個，至少有一人能拿滿、做完、放出全部。$\sum(\text{Max}_i-1)+1$。

@q dl3-16 | os-deadlock#3 | 2 | 找錯
學生算 Banker 的 Need 寫成 Max＋Allocation。正確？
[x] Need＝Max − Allocation：還要多少才到最大需求
[ ] Need＝Allocation − Max
[ ] Need＝Max
[ ] Need＝Available − Allocation
---
Need 就是「最壞情況下還會再要多少」，安全檢查拿它跟 Work 比。

@q dl3-17 | os-deadlock#3 | 2 | 情境
Banker 演算法名字的由來比喻：銀行有固定的現金，客戶有貸款上限。銀行什麼時候不放款？
[x] 放出去之後，就算所有客戶都借到上限，銀行也沒辦法依某個順序讓每個人都借滿、還錢
[ ] 現金不夠的時候
[ ] 客戶信用不好的時候
[ ] 永遠都放款
---
安全狀態＝存在一個順序，讓每個客戶都能借到上限後還錢。

@q dl3-18 | os-deadlock#3 | 3 | 陷阱
系統處於安全狀態，接著允許一個申請之後變成不安全。這時一定會死結嗎？
[x] 不一定：不安全只是「無法保證」，行程實際上可能不會要到最大需求
[ ] 一定
[ ] 一定不會
[ ] 會立刻死結
---
安全 ⊂ 不會死結；不安全裡有一部分會、一部分不會。Banker 保守地拒絕所有可能出事的。

@q dl3-19 | os-deadlock#3 | 2 | 比較
The banker's safety algorithm and the deadlock-detection algorithm have almost the same loop structure. Which of the following statements about them are correct?
[x] The banker's algorithm compares each process against its Need.
[x] Detection compares each process against its current Request.
[x] Both add a finishing process's Allocation back to Work.
[x] Detection asks who is stuck now; the banker's algorithm plans for the worst future.
[ ] Detection never uses Allocation, because only the current Requests decide whether a process can finish.
---
兩者都反覆找「能完成的行程」並「把它的 Allocation 加回 Work」，差別在比較對象：Banker 用「Need」（未來最多還要多少），偵測用「目前的 Request」（現在在等什麼），所以偵測只管「現在誰卡住」。偵測一樣要用 Allocation 歸還資源。

@q dl3-20 | os-deadlock#3 | 3 | 計算
同一種資源 8 個，4 個行程每個最多需要 $k$ 個（$k\ge2$）。$k$ 最大是多少時保證不會死結？
== 2
---
條件 $4(k-1)+1\le8$ ⇒ $k\le2.75$，所以 $k=2$。$k=3$ 時最壞每人拿 2 個共 8 個，全部在等第 3 個。

@q dl4-05 | os-deadlock#4 | 3 | 計算
有兩個環：P1→P2→P3→P1 和 P3→P4→P3（箭頭是「等」）。終止成本 P1 5、P2 4、P3 7、P4 2。要解開所有死結，最小總成本？終止誰？
== 6,P2 P4
== 6,P2,P4
?? 成本,終止誰
---
殺 P3（7）一次破兩個環；或者破第二個環殺 P4（2）、再從第一個環挑最便宜的 P2（4），共 6 更便宜。

@q dl4-06 | os-deadlock#4 | 2 | 觀念
Which of the following are commonly considered when choosing a victim process to terminate or preempt?
[x] Its priority
[x] How long it has already run
[x] How much work it still has left
[x] How many resources it holds and how many more it needs
[ ] The numerical order of process IDs, so that the largest ID is always chosen first
---
目標是讓損失最小，所以看「優先權」「已經跑了多久」「還剩多少」「拿了多少、還要多少」。行程編號和損失無關。

@q dl4-07 | os-deadlock#4 | 3 | 陷阱
每次死結都選「成本最低」的同一個行程當犧牲者，可能出什麼問題？
[x] 那個行程一直被回滾、永遠做不完（飢餓）；要把「被選過幾次」算進成本
[ ] 不會有問題
[ ] 會造成新的死結
[ ] 成本會變負的
---
這跟排程的 aging 是同一個想法。

@q dl4-08 | os-deadlock#4 | 2 | 比較
搶占資源後要 rollback：「全部重來」和「回到檢查點」的取捨？
[x] 全部重來最簡單但浪費最多；回到檢查點損失少，但平時要花成本存狀態
[ ] 兩個一樣
[ ] 檢查點比較浪費
[ ] 全部重來比較省
---
資料庫交易的日誌就是一種讓 rollback 可行的機制。

@q dl4-09 | os-deadlock#4 | 2 | 情境
A system does not run deadlock detection routinely, but only when CPU utilization drops below 40%. Which of the following statements are correct?
[x] Deadlocked processes use no CPU, so many deadlocks make utilization drop.
[x] When detection finally runs, it is hard to tell which request caused the deadlock.
[ ] Low CPU utilization always means that at least one deadlock exists in the system.
[ ] Triggering detection this way prevents deadlocks from forming in the first place.
[ ] This policy runs detection more often than running it on every single request would.
---
死結的行程不用 CPU，「大量死結讓使用率下降」，所以拿來當觸發條件，成本低；但發現時可能已經卡很久，「很難判斷是哪個請求造成的」。使用率低也可能只是工作少；偵測不會預防死結；比起每次請求都檢查，這樣跑得少得多。

@q dl4-10 | os-deadlock#4 | 2 | 找錯
學生說：「偵測到死結後，隨便終止系統裡任何一個行程，死結就解開了。」錯在哪？
[x] 要終止的是死結（環）上的行程；終止無關的行程不會釋放環上需要的資源
[ ] 沒錯
[ ] 要終止所有行程
[ ] 終止行程不能解開死結
---
有多個環時，可能要終止多個行程。

@q dl4-11 | os-deadlock#4 | 3 | 計算
3 個行程形成一個環，每終止一個都要重跑一次偵測演算法（成本 $O(mn^2)$）。「一次終止一個直到解開」最壞要跑幾次偵測（含第一次發現）？
== 3
---
第一次發現死結；終止一個後再偵測（可能還沒解開，例如有多實例資源）；再終止一個後再偵測。一次全部終止只要偵測一次，但損失最大。

@q dl4-12 | os-deadlock#4 | 2 | 情境
A database detects two transactions waiting for each other. Which of the following statements are correct?
?? 複選
[x] It usually aborts one of them, rolls back its changes, and retries it later.
[ ] It usually aborts both transactions, so that neither one keeps any partial changes.
[ ] It usually restarts the whole database server to clear all locks at once.
[ ] It aborts the transaction that has done the most work, since that one holds the most locks.
[ ] It grants both transactions the rows they want at once, since databases allow shared writes.
---
交易本來就設計成可以整個撤銷，所以「中止其中一個、回滾、稍後重試」，另一個就能繼續。中止一個就夠，不必兩個都中止或重開資料庫。通常挑做得最少的，重做代價小。兩個交易同時寫同一列會破壞資料。

@q dl4-13 | os-deadlock#4 | 3 | 比較
「終止一個行程」和「搶占它的某個資源」解除死結，哪個需要額外處理 rollback？
[x] 兩個都要讓被影響的行程回到某個一致的狀態；搶占時行程還活著，要能從被搶之前的狀態繼續，最麻煩
[ ] 只有終止要
[ ] 只有搶占要
[ ] 都不用
---
終止的話整個行程丟掉重跑；搶占的話它還要繼續跑，必須回到拿那個資源之前。

@q dl4-14 | os-deadlock#4 | 1 | 觀念
死結偵測之後，最常見的兩大類復原方式是？
[x] 終止行程、搶占資源
[ ] 重開機、更新系統
[ ] 增加記憶體、增加 CPU
[ ] 改用 Banker、改用排序
---
後兩組是預防或避免的手段，不是發生之後的復原。

@q dl4-15 | os-deadlock#4 | 3 | 情境
三個行程卡在一個環上：P1 已跑 50 分鐘、還剩 1 分鐘；P2 已跑 1 分鐘、還剩 30 分鐘；P3 是使用者正在操作的互動程式。終止誰最合理？
[x] P2：損失的工作最少，又不是互動程式
[ ] P1：它跑最久
[ ] P3：互動程式不重要
[ ] 全部
---
終止 P1 會浪費 50 分鐘；終止 P3 使用者會直接感受到。

@q dl4-16 | os-deadlock#4 | 2 | 計算
資源只有一種、單一實例的情況下，偵測死結就是在等待圖上找環。$n$ 個行程、用相鄰串列存，用 DFS 找環的時間？
[x] $O(n+e)$，$e$ 是等待邊數（最多 $n^2$）
[ ] $O(n!)$
[ ] $O(2^n)$
[ ] $O(1)$
---
多實例時要用類似 Banker 的演算法，$O(mn^2)$。

@q me1-06 | os-memory#1 | 2 | 計算
base 暫存器 4800、limit 1000。某次存取的實體位址是 5600，它的邏輯位址是？
== 800
---
實體＝邏輯＋base，反推 $5600-4800=800$，而且 $800<1000$ 合法。

@q me1-07 | os-memory#1 | 3 | 陷阱
A base/limit system gives a process base 2000 and a byte-length limit of 500, so the process owns physical addresses 2000 through 2499. Every byte of an access must have a logical address below 500; unaligned accesses are allowed, and an access that fails the check is rejected completely, with no bytes read. The process runs a loop that reads four-byte integers starting at logical addresses 488, 492, and 496, and then, because of an off-by-two bug, at 498. A programmer reading the crash report wants to know which of these reads were accepted and why. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The read at 498 covers logical addresses 498 through 501.
[x] The read at 498 must be rejected even though its starting address is in range.
[ ] A four-byte read starting at logical address 497 would fit entirely within the range.
[ ] Checking only whether the starting address is below 500 is enough to approve the read at 498.
[ ] The rejected read at 498 still returns its first two bytes, from physical addresses 2498 and 2499.
---
「498 到 501」對：長度四，最後一個是 498＋4−1。「要拒絕」對：500 與 501 越界。「497 可放」錯：它讀 497 到 500，500 已越界；要從 496 開始（讀 496 到 499）才放得下。「只查起點就夠」錯：還需整個區間合法。「仍回傳前兩個位元組」錯：題設整筆拒絕，一個位元組都不讀。

@q me1-08 | os-memory#1 | 3 | 情境
A deliberately simplified system has no relocation support: the addresses inside a program image are physical addresses fixed when the program is compiled. A program was built to be loaded at base 2000, and one of its instructions loads a counter directly from absolute data address 2300, which is 300 bytes after the start of the image:
```
load  r1, [2300]    ; counter, 300 bytes into the image
```
On a later run the region that starts at 2000 is busy, so the loader copies the unchanged image to base 5000 instead and jumps to its first instruction. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Its embedded data reference is an absolute address.
[x] Loading the image at base 5000 leaves the unchanged instruction referring to 2300.
[ ] Loading it at 5000 automatically changes that stored reference to 5300.
[x] Building an appropriately changed absolute image is one way to run it at the new location.
[x] Execution-time relocation would instead add the current base on every access.
---
「絕對位址」對：實體數字已嵌入指令。「搬到 5000 仍指 2300」對：題設沒有重定位機制。「自行變 5300」錯：那才是新位置中相應資料的地址，但原指令不會自行修補。「重建對應位置的版本」對：可重新產生正確參照。「執行時重定位是每次加 base」對：那是另一種機制，和固定地址不同。

@q me1-09 | os-memory#1 | 1 | 計算
relocation 暫存器 14000，行程大小 3000。它佔用的實體位址範圍最後一個是？
== 16999
---
邏輯 0～2999 對到實體 14000～16999。

@q me1-10 | os-memory#1 | 2 | 找錯
學生說：「MMU 用『邏輯位址 ≥ base』來判斷有沒有越界。」錯在哪？
[x] 要比的是「邏輯位址 < limit」（limit 存的是大小），檢查完再加 base
[ ] 沒錯
[ ] 要比「邏輯位址 < base」
[ ] 不用檢查
---
邏輯位址從 0 開始，跟 base 比沒有意義。

@q me1-11 | os-memory#1 | 1 | 觀念
Two processes run the same compiled program, which reads its configuration word at logical address 256. The operating system has placed them as follows, and each limit is larger than 256:

| process | base | limit |
|---|---|---|
| first | 4096 | 1024 |
| second | 8192 | 2048 |

Whenever the operating system switches from one process to the other, it reloads the base and limit registers with the values in the table. Both processes execute the same instruction, which reads logical address 256, during their first time slice, and neither process ever writes the base or limit registers itself. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The first process reads physical address 4352.
[x] The second process reads physical address 8448.
[ ] Equal logical addresses force equal physical addresses across processes.
[ ] Whichever process runs first determines the other's base value automatically.
[ ] The second process must read physical address 4352 because the instruction's logical address is identical.
---
「4352」對：4096＋256。「8448」對：8192＋256。「邏輯相同必實體相同」錯：還要看各自 base。「先跑者自動決定別人起點」錯：由系統配置與切換時載入的暫存器決定。「第二個也 4352」錯：它的 base 不同，應使用自己的 8192。

@q me1-12 | os-memory#1 | 2 | 比較
靜態連結和動態連結（共享函式庫 .so／.dll）？
[x] 動態連結：執行時才連到函式庫，多個程式共用一份、更新函式庫不用重新編譯；靜態連結：函式庫複製進每個執行檔
[ ] 靜態連結比較省記憶體
[ ] 動態連結不能更新
[ ] 兩者一樣
---
動態連結的代價是執行環境要有正確版本的函式庫（「DLL 地獄」）。

@q me1-13 | os-memory#1 | 2 | 計算
行程 100 MB，磁碟傳輸 50 MB/s。把它整個換出、再換回（swap out＋swap in）至少要幾秒？
== 4
---
各 2 秒。所以整個行程換出換入很貴，現代系統改成以頁為單位。

@q me1-14 | os-memory#1 | 3 | 陷阱
一個行程正在等磁碟把資料直接寫進它記憶體裡的緩衝區。這時把它整個換出到磁碟，換別的行程進來用那塊記憶體，會怎樣？
[x] 磁碟會把資料寫進已經屬於別人的記憶體；所以這種行程不能換出，或 I/O 要先進核心緩衝區
[ ] 沒問題
[ ] 資料會自動跟著換出
[ ] 磁碟會等它換回來
---
DMA 用的是實體位址，換出之後那塊實體記憶體就不是它的了。

@q me1-15 | os-memory#1 | 3 | 觀念
A program initially loads 40 KiB of ordinary code. It also contains a 24 KiB error-handling routine that is not brought in at start-up; the routine is loaded only when it is called for the first time, and it then stays in memory until the program exits. The program is run three times on different inputs: run 1 never reports an error, run 2 reports one error, and run 3 reports five errors. Ignore data, stacks, and all other memory use, and count only these two pieces of code. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[ ] Before the first error-handler call, these code regions already occupy 64 KiB in memory.
[ ] After the routine is loaded, they occupy 88 KiB.
[x] An execution that never calls the routine need not load its 24 KiB.
[ ] Run 3 loads 120 KiB of error-handling code in total, 24 KiB for each of its five errors.
[ ] Delaying the load reduces the routine's own size to 16 KiB.
---
「呼叫前就 64」錯：延後載入的部分還不在記憶體，起初只有 40。「之後 88」錯：40＋24＝64，載入一次就留著。「沒呼叫可不載入」對：這是動態載入降低不必要使用的方式。「run 3 載入 120 KiB」錯：載入一次就留著，後面四次呼叫不用再載，總共只載 24 KiB。「縮成 16」錯：延後載入不會改變程式碼本身大小。

@q me1-16 | os-memory#1 | 3 | 證明
說明為什麼越界檢查一定要由硬體在**每一次**記憶體存取時做，而不能由作業系統事先檢查程式。
---
程式要存取的位址常常在執行時才算出來（指標、陣列索引、使用者輸入），事先看程式碼無法知道；而且每次存取都切到作業系統去檢查太慢。所以由 MMU 硬體在每次存取時比 limit，越界就 trap，作業系統只負責設定 base／limit 和處理 trap。

@q me1-17 | os-memory#1 | 3 | 計算
The operating system switches the CPU from process P1 to process P2. It keeps each process's base and limit values in its own tables:

| process | base | limit |
|---|---|---|
| P1 | 4000 | 1000 |
| P2 | 9000 | 600 |

Only privileged system code may write the base and limit registers. A valid logical address d satisfies $0\le d<\text{limit}$ and translates to base plus d. Right after the switch, P2 reads logical address 500 and then, because of an indexing bug, logical address 700, which is still inside the 1000-byte range that P1 used. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The new process's logical address 500 should translate to 9500.
[x] The system must install the new process's base and limit before allowing its accesses.
[ ] Keeping limit 1000 correctly enforces the new process's assigned 600-byte range.
[ ] Letting user code raise its own limit is harmless, because the base still marks where its region starts.
[ ] Changing the base alone guarantees that logical address 700 is rejected under the old limit.
---
「9500」對：9000＋500。「載入兩個新值」對：起點與合法長度都屬於目前行程。「保留 1000 保護正確」錯：會讓 600 到 999 的不合法位移通過。「使用者自己加大 limit 無害」錯：base 只定起點，終點靠 limit；加大就能讀寫別人的記憶體，所以只有特權程式碼能寫這兩個暫存器。「只改 base 就擋 700」錯：若 limit 仍 1000，700 仍通過範圍檢查。

@q me2-08 | os-memory#2 | 2 | 計算
空洞依序 180、420、90、260、350（KB）；請求依序 250、100、300、80。first-fit 後各空洞剩多少？
== 0 170 90 260 50
== 0,170,90,260,50
---
250→420（剩 170）；100→180（剩 80）；300→350（剩 50）；80→剩 80 的那個（剩 0）。程式模擬驗算。

@q me2-09 | os-memory#2 | 2 | 計算
同一組（空洞 180、420、90、260、350；請求 250、100、300、80），best-fit 後各空洞剩多少？
== 0 420 90 10 50
== 0,420,90,10,50
---
250→260（剩 10，最貼）；100→180（剩 80）；300→350（剩 50）；80→80（剩 0）。420 完整保留，之後來大請求還放得下。

@q me2-10 | os-memory#2 | 3 | 計算
同一組，worst-fit 時哪個請求放不下？
== 300
---
250→420（剩 170）；100→350（剩 250）；300：最大的空洞只剩 260，放不下；80→260（剩 180）。總空間還有很多，卻放不下 300，這就是外部碎片。

@q me2-11 | os-memory#2 | 3 | 追蹤
同一組，next-fit（從上次放的位置往後找，到尾巴繞回開頭）。各請求放在第幾個空洞（從 1 數）？
== 2 2 5 1
== 2,2,5,1
---
250→第 2 個（420）；100 從第 2 個開始找，剩 170 夠 → 第 2 個；300 從第 2 個往後：70、90、260 都不夠，第 5 個 350；80 從第 5 個（剩 50）不夠，繞回第 1 個。程式模擬驗算。

@q me2-12 | os-memory#2 | 1 | 計算
有 3 個空洞各 200 KB（不相鄰），來了一個 300 KB 的請求。放得下嗎？
== 不能
---
總共 600 KB 夠，但沒有一塊連續 300 KB：外部碎片。compaction 把它們合起來就放得下。

@q me2-13 | os-memory#2 | 2 | 計算
固定大小分割區 100、200、300 KB，各放進 90、150、250 KB 的行程。分割區裡面沒用到的空間共多少 KB？
== 110
---
$10+50+50$。分割區內用不到的部分叫內部碎片（下一節分頁也會遇到）。

@q me2-14 | os-memory#2 | 2 | 計算
compaction 要把 200 MB 的行程資料往前搬，記憶體複製速度 1 GB/s。至少要多久（ms）？期間這些行程能跑嗎？
== 200,不能
?? 毫秒,能否執行
---
$200/1000$ 秒。搬動中位址在變，那些行程要暫停。所以 compaction 很貴，而且需要執行時繫結。

@q me2-15 | os-memory#2 | 3 | 找錯
A contiguous allocator keeps its free holes in address order, with these sizes:

| hole | 1 | 2 | 3 |
|---|---|---|---|
| size (KiB) | 180 | 80 | 200 |

A request for 75 KiB arrives and must be placed entirely inside one hole; the rest of the chosen hole stays free. A student compares the three classic placement policies: first-fit takes the first hole in address order that is large enough, best-fit takes the smallest hole that is large enough, and worst-fit takes the largest hole. The student records which hole each policy chooses and how much of that hole is left over. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] First-fit chooses the 180 KiB hole.
[x] Best-fit chooses the 80 KiB hole.
[x] Worst-fit chooses the 200 KiB hole.
[x] Best-fit leaves 5 KiB unused in its chosen hole.
[x] Choosing the first hole that fits in address order is first-fit, not best-fit.
---
「first-fit 選 180」對：它從地址順序找第一個夠大的。「best-fit 選 80」對：這是夠大之中最小的。「worst-fit 選 200」對：它取最大的洞。「剩 5」對：80−75＝5。「第一個夠大的是 first-fit」對：best-fit 要看過全部、挑夠大之中最小的，兩者選擇依據不同。五句都對。

@q me2-16 | os-memory#2 | 3 | 情境
A long-running service uses a contiguous allocator, and after days of allocations and frees its memory contains 4 free holes of 256 KiB each, separated by live blocks that belong to active connections. A new object needs 600 KiB of contiguous memory, and the allocation fails. The live blocks cannot be moved under the current allocation contract, because other code holds raw pointers into them. A developer first suspects a memory leak, and another proposes simply treating two neighboring holes as one. The team must explain the failure in an incident report. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] There are 1024 KiB free in total.
[ ] The 600 KiB allocation is guaranteed to succeed, because more than 600 KiB are free.
[ ] The failure alone proves that some allocated object was never freed due to a memory leak.
[x] Relocation-based compaction would require a way to update or preserve references to moved objects.
[ ] Treating two separated holes as one contiguous hole is valid without changing the intervening allocation.
---
「1024 KiB」對：4×256。「一定成功」錯：最大連續空洞只有 256，600 KiB 的連續請求會失敗。「必洩漏」錯：本題空間是分散，不足以證明程式忘了釋放。「搬動需處理參照」對：否則指標仍指原址；題設也不允許現在任意搬。「隔開可直接當連續」錯：中間還有活著的配置，不能越過它當成同一洞。

@q me2-17 | os-memory#2 | 3 | 比較
worst-fit 的想法是「挑最大的空洞，剩下的還夠大、可以給別人用」。實驗結果通常？
[x] 在速度和使用率上都不如 first-fit 和 best-fit：大的空洞很快被切碎，大請求反而沒地方放
[ ] 一定最好
[ ] 跟 best-fit 一樣
[ ] 沒有外部碎片
---
上面那組例子 worst-fit 就放不下 300，first-fit 和 best-fit 都放得下。

@q me3-21 | os-memory#3 | 2 | 觀念
內部碎片和外部碎片的差別？
[x] 內部：配給行程的區塊裡沒用到的部分；外部：區塊之間零散的空洞，總和夠但不連續
[ ] 兩者一樣
[ ] 內部只發生在連續配置
[ ] 外部只發生在分頁
---
固定大小配置（分頁、固定分割）有內部碎片；可變大小配置（連續配置、分段）有外部碎片。

@q me2-19 | os-memory#2 | 3 | 證明
說明為什麼 compaction 需要「執行時位址繫結」。
---
compaction 會在行程執行到一半時把它搬到別的實體位置。若是編譯時或載入時繫結，程式裡的位址已經是實體位址，搬過去之後所有指標都錯了；執行時繫結則程式只用邏輯位址，搬動後改一下 base 暫存器就好。

@q me3-09 | os-memory#3 | 2 | 計算
頁大小 1 KB，邏輯位址 3085。頁號、位移？若第 3 頁在第 5 個 frame，實體位址？
== 3,13,5133
?? 頁號,位移,實體位址
---
$3085=3\times1024+13$；實體 $5\times1024+13=5133$。

@q me3-10 | os-memory#3 | 2 | 計算
32 位元位址、頁 4 KB。邏輯位址 0x12345678 的頁號和位移（用 0x 開頭的 hex 寫）？
== 0x12345,0x678
== 12345,678
?? 頁號,位移
---
頁 4 KB＝$2^{12}$，位移是最低 12 個位元＝3 個 16 進位數字；剩下的是頁號。

@q me3-11 | os-memory#3 | 3 | 找錯
A paging system uses 4096-byte pages, so the low 12 bits of a virtual address give the page offset and the remaining bits give the virtual page number. A program accesses a virtual address whose page number is 5 and whose page offset is 123, that is, virtual address $5\times4096+123 = 20603$. The page table maps virtual page 5 to physical frame 9. A student computes the physical address, and a classmate proposes adding the frame number and the offset directly, or reusing the virtual page number. Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The physical address is 36987.
[x] The page offset stays 123 after translation.
[ ] Adding frame number and offset directly gives the physical address 132.
[ ] The start of frame 9 is physical address 32768.
[ ] Using virtual page number 5 instead of mapped frame number 9 gives the same physical address.
---
「36987」對：9×4096＋123。「位移保留」對：分頁轉換換掉的是頁號對應的框號。「132」錯：9＋123 少了框號乘頁大小。「框起點 32768」錯：那是 8×4096，框 8 的起點；框 9 從 36864 開始。「用虛頁 5 結果相同」錯：5×4096＋123＝20603，和框 9 的地址不同。

@q me3-12 | os-memory#3 | 1 | 計算
實體記憶體 1 GB、frame 4 KB。有幾個 frame？
== 262144
---
$2^{30}/2^{12}=2^{18}$。

@q me3-13 | os-memory#3 | 2 | 情境
30 個使用者同時用同一個編輯器，程式碼 120 KB（唯讀、可共用）、每人資料 40 KB。不共用和共用程式碼頁，各要多少 KB？
== 4800,1320
?? 不共用,共用
---
不共用 $30\times160$；共用 $120+30\times40$。分頁讓多個行程的頁表指到同一個 frame 就能共用。

@q me3-14 | os-memory#3 | 3 | 計算
平均行程大小 $s=1$ MB、頁表每項 $e=8$ bytes。考慮頁表大小 $\frac sp e$ 和平均內部碎片 $\frac p2$，總浪費最小的頁大小 $p$？
== 4096
---
$\frac{se}p+\frac p2$ 對 $p$ 微分為 0：$p=\sqrt{2se}=\sqrt{2\cdot2^{20}\cdot8}=2^{12}$。

@q me3-15 | os-memory#3 | 3 | 觀念
A process has space for 8 entries in a single-level page table. Only virtual pages 0 through 5 are part of its permitted address space, and those 6 entries are valid and resident. Entries 6 and 7 exist as storage positions in the table but are marked invalid. Page 7 is explicitly outside the process's permitted space; this is not a temporarily absent page waiting to be brought from a file.

The page size is 4096 bytes. A user instruction attempts to read at page 7 with offset 16, which is a perfectly ordinary offset inside a page. Ignore the TLB and assume the processor checks the selected entry's validity before allowing the access. A student argues that allocating 8 entry positions automatically authorizes all page numbers that can index those positions. Another proposes returning 0 without reporting the invalid reference. Evaluate those claims without confusing a table's allocated storage with the address-space policy encoded by its entries. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[x] Page 7 must not be accepted merely because an entry slot numbered 7 exists.
[ ] An access to page 7 is silently ignored, so the operating system is never involved.
[ ] The reference must return zero because the page is not permitted.
[ ] Eight page entries are valid under the stated setup.
[ ] The existence of eight table slots makes all eight virtual pages legal automatically.
---
「有表格位置不等於合法」對：還要看映射與保護資訊。「默默忽略、不通知系統」錯：硬體發現無效項會產生例外，交給作業系統處理，不能直接執行未允許的存取。「必回零」錯：非法存取沒有這種預設資料值。「有效八項」錯：只有 0 到 5 共六項有效。「八格就八頁合法」錯：表可包含無效項。

@q me3-16 | os-memory#3 | 3 | 觀念
A user process has a valid resident mapping for 1 4096-byte page. Its entry permits reads but forbids writes. The relevant bytes begin at virtual address 0x3000, and a user-mode instruction attempts to store 8 bytes starting at address 0x3080. The complete store lies within this page, so no second mapping is consulted. The existing data are present in physical memory and may already be cached.

There is no copy-on-write policy, no handler that grants additional permission, and no instruction that changes the page's protection for this experiment. The processor must enforce the page-entry permission independently of whether the requested data are resident. A debugger shows that translation succeeds and a student takes this as proof that the later write must also succeed. Decide whether a successful address translation, a valid entry and permission for the attempted operation are equivalent properties. Do not assume that read-only memory silently creates a writable replacement. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The write must fail the page-permission check.
[x] The processor notifies the operating system of the protection violation.
[ ] The instruction may silently modify the eight bytes merely because the page is resident.
[x] A valid mapping and write permission are separate properties.
[x] Without such a policy, read-only permission by itself does not create a writable private copy.
---
「寫入檢查失敗」對：有效且在記憶體，不代表可寫。「保護例外」對：系統需要處理不合法的使用者存取。「可偷偷改八位元組」錯：會違反頁面保護。「有效與可寫分開」對：一項映射可有效但唯讀。「唯讀本身不會自動複製」對：自動複製是另外的寫時複製政策，題設沒有。

@q me3-17 | os-memory#3 | 3 | 陷阱
A TLB lookup takes 4 ns and each memory access takes 80 ns. A single-level table miss requires 1 table access and 1 data access; all required data are in main memory. Model S completes the TLB lookup before starting memory. In model O, the TLB lookup and table access start together, and on a TLB miss the data access starts after the table access. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[ ] Under model S, a TLB miss takes 160 ns.
[ ] Under model O, a TLB miss takes 164 ns.
[ ] Model S always omits the 4 ns lookup from the time of a TLB miss, since that lookup failed.
[x] Whether lookup time is added depends on the specified overlap model.
[ ] Both models have a TLB-miss time of 84 ns.
---
「循序 160 ns」錯：循序要先查完 TLB，4＋80＋80＝164。「重疊 164 ns」錯：前段 4 與 80 同時開始，只花 80，再讀資料 80，共 160。兩個數字剛好對調。「循序不加四」錯：它明定先查完才開始。「依是否重疊」對：不能只背一個式子。「84 ns」錯：那只算查 TLB 加一次記憶體，漏掉 miss 時的另一筆查表或取資料。

@q me3-18 | os-memory#3 | 3 | 情境
2 address spaces each use virtual page number 2, but their page tables give it different physical homes. Address-space identifier 7 maps that page to physical frame 9; identifier 8 maps it to frame thirteen. Pages and frames contain 4096 bytes. A TLB currently retains the translation associated with identifier 7 when the processor switches to the process using identifier 8.

The next reference uses page 2 with offset 1 hundred. Compare a lookup that tests only the virtual page number with 1 that also tests the address-space identifier. As an alternative design, consider a TLB with no identifiers whose entries are all cleard at every address-space switch. Finally, the operating system has only a finite supply of identifiers and may later reuse a number for an unrelated address space. Old translations are not automatically correct merely because their stored identifier has the same numeric value. Evaluate what each design must distinguish or clear. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Comparing only virtual page number 2 can select the wrong translation.
[x] Checking an address-space identifier can distinguish the two entries.
[ ] Identifier 8 must use frame 9 because the virtual page number is unchanged.
[ ] Flushing the old untagged translations cannot prevent use of the old mapping.
[ ] An identifier may always be reassigned to an unrelated address space without considering old entries bearing that identifier.
---
「只比頁號會錯」對：可能誤取 frame 9，正確應是 13。「加識別碼可區分」對：識別碼 7 的表項不能當成 8 的命中。「仍 frame 9」錯：不同空間可有不同映射。「清掉舊項也沒用」錯：切換時把沒標識別碼的舊映射清掉，就不會沿用，這是另一種常見做法。「任意重用識別碼」錯：重新指派前須處理可能殘留的同識別碼舊映射。

@q me3-19 | os-memory#3 | 3 | 計算
TLB 查詢 20 ns、記憶體 100 ns（循序：先查 TLB）。命中率從 80% 提高到 98%，有效存取時間從多少降到多少？
== 140,122
?? 80%,98%
---
命中 $20+100=120$，沒中 $20+100+100=220$。$0.8\cdot120+0.2\cdot220=140$；$0.98\cdot120+0.02\cdot220=122$。

@q me3-20 | os-memory#3 | 1 | 觀念
A single-level page table is stored in ordinary memory using 8-byte entries. PTBR is a processor register containing the starting address of the currently selected table. The hardware reads entry p from PTBR plus 8 times p, then uses the contents of that entry to continue translation. The address of the entry and the physical frame number stored inside it are different quantities.

Before a process switch, PTBR contains 4096. The operating system saves the old execution state and loads 8192 into PTBR for the new process. Both tables remain in memory, and the new table has already been initialized. Ignore the TLB and all lookup caching. Compare the memory location used for entry 3 before and after the switch. A proposed alternative would copy the entire page table into processor registers, but this is not how the specified design works. Determine what the 1 base register changes and what information can stay in memory. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Page-table entry 3 is now read at address 8216.
[x] Changing PTBR changes the table used for subsequent lookups.
[x] The processor need not copy the complete new page table into its registers on a switch.
[x] Under the old PTBR, entry 3 was read at address 4120.
[x] The new table can remain in memory while PTBR holds its base address.
---
「8216」對：8192＋8×3＝8216。「改 PTBR 改查表來源」對：後續計算以新的表起點為基準。「不必整張複製」對：頁表留在記憶體。「舊的是 4120」對：4096＋24。「只存表起點」對：暫存器不需容納整張頁表。五句都對。

@q me4-09 | os-memory#4 | 2 | 計算
36 位元邏輯位址、頁 8 KB、每項 4 bytes。單層頁表多大（MB）？
== 32
---
頁號 $36-13=23$ 位元，$2^{23}$ 項 × 4 bytes $=2^{25}$ bytes $=32$ MB。

@q me4-10 | os-memory#4 | 3 | 計算
32 位元、頁 4 KB、每項 4 bytes、兩層（10/10/12）。行程只用了位址空間最底下 8 MB（程式和資料）和最頂端 4 MB（堆疊）。頁表總共佔多少 KB？
== 16
---
每張內層頁表管 $2^{10}$ 頁 × 4 KB＝4 MB。8 MB 要 2 張、4 MB 要 1 張，加 1 張外層，共 4 張 × 4 KB。單層要 4 MB。

@q me4-11 | os-memory#4 | 3 | 計算
兩層頁表、TLB 查詢 10 ns、記憶體 100 ns、命中率 90%（循序）。有效存取時間？
== 130
---
命中 $10+100$；沒中 $10+100+100+100$（兩層頁表各一次再取資料）＝310。$0.9\cdot110+0.1\cdot310=130$。

@q me4-12 | os-memory#4 | 3 | 陷阱
「多層頁表一定比單層省空間」對嗎？
[ ] 對
[x] 不對
---
省空間是因為沒用到的區域不用配內層頁表。如果行程把整個位址空間都用滿，多層還要多一層外層表，反而比較大；而且每層都多一次記憶體存取。

@q me4-13 | os-memory#4 | 2 | 計算
實體記憶體 8 GB、frame 8 KB，反轉頁表每項 8 bytes（行程 ID＋頁號）。表多大（MB）？
== 8
---
$2^{33}/2^{13}=2^{20}$ 項 × 8 bytes。大小只跟實體記憶體有關，不管有幾個行程。

@q me4-14 | os-memory#4 | 3 | 比較
An operating system compares ordinary per-process page tables with a simplified inverted table. There are 1024 physical frames, and each basic inverted-table entry occupies 16 bytes. The inverted table has exactly 1 entry per physical frame and records only 1 process/virtual-page pair in that entry. Do not silently assume that a list of additional aliases is already included in those 16 bytes.

4 distinct pairs need to refer to physical frame 9: 1 process uses virtual page 3, another uses page 7, a third uses page 8, and a fourth uses page fourteen. The requirement is to share the same physical data, rather than to create 4 independent copies of the page. The designer may consider adding information to describe sharing, but the question asks what the stated basic representation can already express. Compare the table's fixed storage with the alias information required by this example, and with the ability of separate ordinary page tables to point at 1 frame. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The basic inverted table occupies 16384 bytes.
[x] One stored pair alone cannot represent all four sharing aliases.
[ ] Sharing inherently requires four separate copies of the frame's data.
[ ] Ordinary per-process tables cannot represent sharing, since a frame may appear in only one of them.
[ ] The basic inverted table has one entry for every virtual page of every process.
---
「16384」對：1024×16。「單一配對不夠」對：四組不同的行程與虛頁對應同一實體框，需要額外處理別名。「必四份資料」錯：共享正是要讓多組映射指同一份資料。「一般頁表不能共享」錯：一般頁表可以讓好幾個行程的表項指向同一框，直接表示共享。「每個虛頁一項」錯：反轉表的基本索引是實體框，一框一項。

@q me4-16 | os-memory#4 | 3 | 計算
x86-64 用 48 位元虛擬位址、4 層頁表（9/9/9/9/12）。改用 2 MB 大頁時，查到第幾層就得到實體位址？
== 3
---
2 MB＝$2^{21}$，位移變成 21 位元＝原本最後一層的 9 位元＋12。只要走前三層，第三層的項目直接指向 2 MB 的 frame。

@q me4-17 | os-memory#4 | 3 | 找錯
A teaching machine uses 32-bit virtual byte addresses divided, from high to low, into a ten-bit outer index, a ten-bit inner index and a 12-bit page offset. Each entry in either table level occupies 4 bytes. A complete outer table includes every entry that its index can select, and an allocated inner table likewise includes every entry selected by the inner index. Ignore headers and allocation metadata.

An implementation report mixes up 3 quantities: the number of entries, the number of bytes occupied by those entries, and the number of data bytes addressed by the page offset. It claims that a ten-bit index gives 1024 bytes of table storage and that the offset bits determine the outer table's entry count. As a concrete reference, address 0x00401010 must first be split according to the stated bit widths, not according to byte-sized hexadecimal groups. Check the structural counts before accepting the report. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[ ] The outer table contains 1024 entries, while each inner table contains 4096 entries.
[ ] A complete outer table occupies 1024 bytes.
[x] Each inner table contains 1024 entries.
[ ] The outer table contains 4096 entries because the offset has twelve bits.
[ ] Each page has only 1024 bytes because the outer index has ten bits.
---
「內表 4096 項」錯、「內表 1024 項」對：兩層各用十位索引，2^10＝1024。「外表 1024 位元組」錯：1024 項×4＝4096 位元組。「外表 4096 項」錯：把頁內位移位數誤當外表索引。「頁 1024 位元組」錯：頁大小由十二位位移決定，是 2^12＝4096 位元組。

@q me4-18 | os-memory#4 | 2 | 證明
說明多層頁表為什麼通常讓「每一張頁表剛好一頁大」。
---
頁表本身也存在記憶體裡、也要被分頁管理。剛好一頁時，一張表正好放進一個 frame，配置和換出都以頁為單位，不需要連續的大空間；若超過一頁又要連續存放，就回到連續配置的碎片問題。所以每層的索引位元數＝log₂(頁大小／每項大小)。

@q me4-19 | os-memory#4 | 1 | 計算
頁 4 KB、每項 8 bytes，一張頁表剛好一頁時，每層能用幾個位元當索引？
== 9
---
一頁放 $4096/8=512=2^9$ 項。

@q me5-05 | os-memory#5 | 2 | 計算

合法的位移是 0～299，等於 limit 就已經超出。邊界條件是 $d<\text{limit}$。

@q me5-08 | os-memory#5 | 3 | 計算

@q me5-09 | os-memory#5 | 3 | 比較
A program contains 2000 bytes of code and 1000 bytes of stack data. In 1 design, segmentation represents these as 2 separate variable-sized contiguous segments. Each segment has its own length and permission information. Code may be read-only while the stack remains writable; the 2 logical units need not have identical lengths merely because they belong to 1 program.

For comparison, a specified paging layout reserves 1 separate 4096-byte page for the code and another separate page for the stack. Neither page contains data from another allocation, and unused bytes inside these reserved pages cannot be reassigned in this comparison. Ignore all metadata and alignment overhead in the segmentation model. Later allocations and releases may leave separate holes among live variable-sized segments, and no rule guarantees that those holes join into 1 continuous region. Compare logical organization, protection and the memory reservation of this exact paging layout without claiming that either method eliminates every form of fragmentation. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Segments can reflect the program's code and stack as distinct logical units.
[x] Different permissions can be assigned to the two segments.
[x] The stated paging layout reserves 8192 bytes for 3000 bytes of contents.
[ ] Segmentation requires all segments to be exactly 4096 bytes long.
[x] Variable-sized segments can still leave external fragmentation.
---
「邏輯單位」對：段可配合程式碼、堆疊等結構。「不同權限」對：各段有自己的描述與保護設定。「8192」對：題設各占一頁，2×4096＝8192；未使用部分為 5192。「固定 4096」錯：這是分頁的固定切法，不是分段的必要條件。「仍可能外部碎片」對：不同長度的連續段可能把空間切成許多小洞。

@q me5-10 | os-memory#5 | 3 | 找錯
A segment descriptor contains physical base 6000 and byte-length limit 500. For this descriptor the permitted offsets are defined by 0 less than or equal to d and d strictly less than 500. Only after that test succeeds does the processor form the physical byte address by adding the base to the offset. The fact that the resulting number falls somewhere in installed memory does not replace the segment-bound check.

A debugger records attempted offsets 499, 500, 5999 and negative 1. A student instead accepts every nonnegative offset numerically smaller than the base, and another student exchanges the meanings of the 2 descriptor values. Treat the base as the beginning of the segment and the limit as its length, with no hidden inclusive endpoint. Ignore read/write permission differences so that this exercise isolates address bounds. Determine which conclusions follow from the correct test, especially at the final byte and at the first excluded offset. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] Offset 499 is valid and translates to address 6499.
[x] Offset 500 is outside this segment.
[ ] Every offset less than base 6000 is valid.
[ ] The base records the segment's length, while the limit records where it starts.
[ ] Offset 5999 is valid because it is below the base.
---
「499 有效」對：499＜500，6000＋499＝6499。「500 越界」對：題設長度上界不包含自身。「小於 base 就有效」錯：base 是實體起點，不是合法位移上限。「base 是長度、limit 是起點」錯：剛好對調，base 是起點、limit 是長度。「5999 有效」錯：它早已超過長度 500。

@q me5-11 | os-memory#5 | 3 | 觀念

| Region | Size in KiB | State |
|---|---|---|
| first original segment | 120 | live |
| released middle segment | 80 | free |
| last original segment | 120 | live |
| separate region | 60 | free |

A new request needs 1 segment containing 100 contiguous KiB. Existing live segments may not move, and the requested segment may not be divided among holes. Coalescing is allowed only when free regions are physically adjacent. Decide whether adding the sizes in the free-space total establishes a legal placement, and whether releasing the middle segment changes ownership of its live neighbors. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
?? 複選
[ ] The two holes contain 200 KiB in total.
[ ] The request fits once the 60 KiB hole is added to the 80 KiB hole.
[ ] Releasing the middle segment makes the two outer live segments free as well.
[x] Variable-sized contiguous segments can leave external fragmentation.
[ ] The allocator may count the occupied outer segments as part of one free 140 KiB region.
---
「共 200」錯：80＋60＝140。「兩個洞加起來就放得下」錯：兩個洞不相鄰，100 KiB 要放在單一連續空間，兩個都比 100 小。「兩旁也空了」錯：題設只釋放中段，外段仍活著。「可有外部碎片」對：分段並未取消連續配置限制。「占用處也算空洞」錯：不能把活著的段當作可配置空間。

@q me5-12 | os-memory#5 | 3 | 情境
2 processes execute identical shared code bytes containing an explicit logical jump to segment number 2 at offset 1 hundred. There is no runtime rewriting of that numeric segment operand and no special hardware search for whichever segment happens to contain shared code. Each process consults its own segment table when executing the instruction.

| Process | Segment number | Contents | Physical base |
|---|---|---|---|
| X | 2 | shared code | 50000 |
| Y | 2 | private data | 90000 |
| Y | 5 | the same shared code | 50000 |

All listed segments are long enough for the offset and, solely to isolate address selection in this example, their permissions allow the attempted jump. Therefore the experiment is about which descriptor is chosen, not a protection failure. Compare giving the code a consistent segment number with changing only its physical placement. Do not assume that identical instruction bytes automatically acquire different embedded segment numbers in different processes. Which statements are correct? Select all that apply.
[x] The unchanged jump selects segment 2 in both processes.
[x] In process Y, the jump does not select shared-code segment 5 automatically.
[ ] Equal physical code bytes guarantee that an embedded segment number has the same intended meaning in every process.
[ ] Giving the shared code the same segment number in every process cannot help, because each process has its own segment table.
[ ] Changing only the shared code's physical base necessarily rewrites the jump's embedded segment number from 2 to 5.
---
「兩者都選段 2」對：指令明寫 2。「不自動選段 5」對：在第二個行程裡會選到私有資料段，並非原意的程式碼。「相同碼就語意必同」錯：段號要配合各行程的段表。「用一致的段號沒幫助」錯：各行程都把共享碼放在同一段號，未改的跳躍指令就到處都對；這正是常見做法（或改採其他合適的參照方式）。「改 base 就改指令段號」錯：描述的實體位置與指令內的段號是不同資訊。

@q me5-13 | os-memory#5 | 1 | 觀念
分段的段表每一項至少要有哪兩個欄位？
[x] base（起點）和 limit（長度）
[ ] 頁號和 frame
[ ] 行程 ID 和優先權
[ ] 讀和寫
---
通常還有保護位元（讀／寫／執行）和 valid 位元。

@q me5-14 | os-memory#5 | 3 | 情境
